     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * review-chunker.cjs — review a subject too large for one reviewer call.
     4	 *
     5	 * THE PROBLEM THIS SOLVES
     6	 * On 2026-08-25 a Codex review of 452,768 bytes across 41 paths hit the 600s
     7	 * ceiling and returned nothing. The obvious "fix" — review less — is not a fix,
     8	 * it is a smaller claim dressed as the same one. The subject must still be
     9	 * covered completely; it just cannot be covered in a single call.
    10	 *
    11	 * So the subject is split into deterministic, COHERENT groups, each reviewed
    12	 * within its own bounded call, and the group verdicts are aggregated under
    13	 * rules strict enough that the aggregate is worth no more than the groups
    14	 * beneath it.
    15	 *
    16	 * DETERMINISM
    17	 * The same subject always produces the same groups. Grouping is a pure function
    18	 * of the sorted path list — no timestamps, no randomness, no balancing that
    19	 * depends on machine speed. If it were not deterministic, a group record could
    20	 * never be matched back to the plan it came from.
    21	 *
    22	 * COHERENCE
    23	 * Paths are grouped by ROLE, not by chopping a sorted list into equal slices. A
    24	 * reviewer handed "the routing policy plus its router plus its adapters" can
    25	 * reason about a mechanism; one handed "eight files that happened to sort
    26	 * together" can only pattern-match.
    27	 *
    28	 * WHAT MAKES THE AGGREGATE CONSUMABLE
    29	 * The gate accepts an aggregate ONLY when all of the following hold:
    30	 *   - the union of group paths equals the subject EXACTLY
    31	 *   - no path appears in two groups (overlap hides disagreement)
    32	 *   - no group carries a path outside the subject (foreign evidence)
    33	 *   - every group is bound to the same subject hash
    34	 *   - every group shares one reviewer identity AND one model
    35	 *   - every group record is signed and verifies
    36	 *   - no group is UNAVAILABLE
    37	 * Any failure produces an UNAVAILABLE aggregate with the reason named. There is
    38	 * no "mostly covered".
    39	 *
    40	 *   node builder-control/review-chunker.cjs --plan   [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--groups N] [--json]
    41	 *   node builder-control/review-chunker.cjs --run    --group <id> --packet <p> [--timeout <s>]
    42	 *   node builder-control/review-chunker.cjs --run-all --packet <p> [--timeout <s>]
    43	 *   node builder-control/review-chunker.cjs --aggregate --packet <p> [--json]
    44	 *
    45	 * Exit: 0 ok · 2 usage · 3 refused / incomplete coverage
    46	 */
    47	'use strict';
    48	
    49	const fs = require('fs');
    50	const path = require('path');
    51	const crypto = require('crypto');
    52	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    53	
    54	const HERE = __dirname;
    55	const ROOT = path.resolve(HERE, '..');
    56	const ENGOS = path.join(HERE, 'engineering-os.cjs');
    57	const ADAPTERS = path.join(HERE, 'review-adapters.cjs');
    58	const REVIEWS_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
    59	const GROUPS_DIR = path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, 'groups');
    60	const RAW_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'review-raw');
    61	
    62	const EXIT_PASS = 0;
    63	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    64	const EXIT_REFUSED = 3;
    65	
    66	const DEFAULT_GROUPS = 5;
    67	const sha256 = (s) => crypto.createHash('sha256').update(s).digest('hex');
    68	
    69	// ── coherence classification ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    70	// Ordered, first-match-wins. Order is part of the contract: moving a rule
    71	// changes grouping for every future subject, so it is deliberately explicit
    72	// rather than derived from directory depth.
    73	const ROLES = [
    74	  { id: 'tests',        re: /(^|\/)test\//,                                    label: 'Tests and fixtures' },
    75	  { id: 'specs-docs',   re: /(^|\/)(specs?|docs?)\/|\.md$/i,                    label: 'Specifications and doctrine' },
    76	  { id: 'ci',           re: /^\.github\//,                                      label: 'CI and reviewer instructions' },
    77	  { id: 'projection',   re: /(aegis-state|knowledge-mirror|connector-registry)|(^|\/)(dashboard|hosting)\//, label: 'Projection, dashboard and hosting' },
    78	  { id: 'routing',      re: /(tool-router|review-adapters|review-sign|review-chunker|MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY|TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON)/, label: 'Model routing and reviewer adapters' },
    79	  { id: 'control-core', re: /(^|\/)builder-control\//,                          label: 'Control core: gate, ledger, packets' },
    80	  { id: 'other',        re: /.*/,                                               label: 'Everything else' },
    81	];
    82	
    83	function roleOf(p) {
    84	  for (const r of ROLES) if (r.re.test(p)) return r.id;
    85	  return 'other';
    86	}
    87	
    88	/**
    89	 * Deterministic grouping. Pure function of the sorted path list.
    90	 *
    91	 * Roles are emitted in ROLES order. If there are more populated roles than the
    92	 * target count, the SMALLEST adjacent roles merge (smallest first keeps the
    93	 * large coherent roles intact). If there are fewer, the largest role splits on
    94	 * a stable sort — never on file size, which would vary between machines.
    95	 */
    96	function planGroups(subjectPaths, target = DEFAULT_GROUPS) {
    97	  const paths = [...new Set(subjectPaths)].sort();
    98	  if (!paths.length) return [];
    99	
   100	  const byRole = new Map();
   101	  for (const p of paths) {
   102	    const r = roleOf(p);
   103	    if (!byRole.has(r)) byRole.set(r, []);
   104	    byRole.get(r).push(p);
   105	  }
   106	
   107	  let groups = ROLES
   108	    .filter((r) => byRole.has(r.id))
   109	    .map((r) => ({ role: r.id, label: r.label, paths: byRole.get(r.id).slice().sort() }));
   110	
   111	  // Too many groups: merge the smallest pair repeatedly. Ties break on role
   112	  // order, so the outcome does not depend on Map iteration order.
   113	  while (groups.length > target) {
   114	    let bestI = 0, bestSize = Infinity;
   115	    for (let i = 0; i < groups.length - 1; i++) {
   116	      const size = groups[i].paths.length + groups[i + 1].paths.length;
   117	      if (size < bestSize) { bestSize = size; bestI = i; }
   118	    }
   119	    const merged = {
   120	      role: `${groups[bestI].role}+${groups[bestI + 1].role}`,
   121	      label: `${groups[bestI].label} + ${groups[bestI + 1].label}`,
   122	      paths: [...groups[bestI].paths, ...groups[bestI + 1].paths].sort(),
   123	    };
   124	    groups.splice(bestI, 2, merged);
   125	  }
   126	
   127	  // Too few groups: split the largest in half on the stable sort.
   128	  while (groups.length < target && groups.some((g) => g.paths.length > 1)) {
   129	    let bigI = 0;
   130	    for (let i = 1; i < groups.length; i++) {
   131	      if (groups[i].paths.length > groups[bigI].paths.length) bigI = i;
   132	    }
   133	    if (groups[bigI].paths.length < 2) break;
   134	    const half = Math.ceil(groups[bigI].paths.length / 2);
   135	    const a = { ...groups[bigI], paths: groups[bigI].paths.slice(0, half) };
   136	    const b = { ...groups[bigI], role: groups[bigI].role + '-b', label: groups[bigI].label + ' (part 2)', paths: groups[bigI].paths.slice(half) };
   137	    a.label = a.label.endsWith('(part 2)') ? a.label : a.label + ' (part 1)';
   138	    a.role = a.role.endsWith('-b') ? a.role : a.role + '-a';
   139	    groups.splice(bigI, 1, a, b);
   140	  }
   141	
   142	  return groups.map((g, i) => ({
   143	    groupId: `G${i + 1}`,
   144	    role: g.role,
   145	    label: g.label,
   146	    paths: g.paths,
   147	    pathCount: g.paths.length,
   148	    groupDigest: sha256(g.paths.join('\n')),
   149	  }));
   150	}
   151	
   152	// ── subject ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   153	function subjectOf(args) {
   154	  const a = [ENGOS, '--subject', '--json'];
   155	  if (args.base) a.push('--base', args.base);
   156	  if (args.head) a.push('--head', args.head);
   157	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   158	  if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`could not compute subject: ${(r.stderr || '').trim()}`);
   159	  return JSON.parse(r.stdout);
   160	}
   161	
   162	function groupBytes(g, args) {
   163	  const a = ['diff'];
   164	  if (args.base) a.push(`${args.base}..${args.head || 'HEAD'}`);
   165	  else a.push(args.head || 'HEAD');
   166	  a.push('--', ...g.paths);
   167	  const r = spawnSync('git', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 256 * 1024 * 1024 });
   168	  return r.status === 0 ? r.stdout.length : 0;
   169	}
   170	
   171	function cmdPlan(args) {
   172	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   173	  const target = Number(args.groups) > 0 ? Number(args.groups) : DEFAULT_GROUPS;
   174	  const groups = planGroups(subject.subjectPaths, target);
   175	
   176	  // Coverage is asserted on the PLAN too, not only on the evidence. A planner
   177	  // that could emit an incomplete plan would produce group records that can
   178	  // never aggregate, and the failure would surface hours later.
   179	  const cov = checkCoverage(groups, subject.subjectPaths);
   180	  if (!cov.ok) throw new Error(`the plan does not cover the subject: ${cov.reason}`);
   181	
   182	  const out = {
   183	    subjectSha256: subject.subjectSha256,
   184	    subjectPathCount: subject.subjectPaths.length,
   185	    groupCount: groups.length,
   186	    groups: groups.map((g) => ({ ...g, diffBytes: groupBytes(g, args) })),
   187	  };
   188	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(out, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
   189	
   190	  console.log('AEGIS — CHUNKED REVIEW PLAN');
   191	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   192	  console.log(`subject : ${out.subjectSha256}`);
   193	  console.log(`paths   : ${out.subjectPathCount} across ${out.groupCount} group(s)`);
   194	  console.log('');
   195	  for (const g of out.groups) {
   196	    console.log(`${g.groupId}  ${g.label}`);
   197	    console.log(`    ${g.pathCount} path(s), ${g.diffBytes} diff bytes`);
   198	    for (const p of g.paths) console.log(`      ${p}`);
   199	    console.log('');
   200	  }
   201	  console.log('Coverage: exact — every subject path appears in exactly one group.');
   202	  return EXIT_PASS;
   203	}
   204	
   205	// ── coverage: the whole point ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   206	function checkCoverage(groups, subjectPaths) {
   207	  const subject = new Set(subjectPaths);
   208	  const seen = new Map();
   209	  const foreign = [];
   210	  const overlap = [];
   211	
   212	  for (const g of groups) {
   213	    for (const p of g.paths) {
   214	      if (!subject.has(p)) { foreign.push({ path: p, groupId: g.groupId }); continue; }
   215	      if (seen.has(p)) overlap.push({ path: p, groups: [seen.get(p), g.groupId] });
   216	      else seen.set(p, g.groupId);
   217	    }
   218	  }
   219	  const missing = [...subject].filter((p) => !seen.has(p));
   220	
   221	  if (missing.length) {
   222	    return { ok: false, code: 'COVERAGE-GAP', reason: `${missing.length} subject path(s) are in no group: ${missing.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}${missing.length > 5 ? ' …' : ''}` };
   223	  }
   224	  if (overlap.length) {
   225	    return { ok: false, code: 'COVERAGE-OVERLAP', reason: `${overlap.length} path(s) appear in more than one group (${overlap.slice(0, 3).map((o) => `${o.path} in ${o.groups.join(' and ')}`).join('; ')}). Overlap lets two reviewers disagree about the same code without the disagreement being visible.` };
   226	  }
   227	  if (foreign.length) {
   228	    return { ok: false, code: 'COVERAGE-FOREIGN', reason: `${foreign.length} path(s) are reviewed but not in the subject: ${foreign.slice(0, 5).map((f) => `${f.path} (${f.groupId})`).join(', ')}` };
   229	  }
   230	  return { ok: true, covered: seen.size };
   231	}
   232	
   233	// ── per-group review ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   234	function cmdRun(args) {
   235	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   236	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   237	  const groups = planGroups(subject.subjectPaths, Number(args.groups) > 0 ? Number(args.groups) : DEFAULT_GROUPS);
   238	  const wanted = args.group
   239	    ? groups.filter((g) => g.groupId === args.group)
   240	    : groups;
   241	  if (args.group && !wanted.length) return usage(`unknown group ${args.group}`);
   242	
   243	  fs.mkdirSync(GROUPS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   244	  let worst = 0;
   245	  for (const g of wanted) {
   246	    console.log(`\n── ${g.groupId}  ${g.label}  (${g.pathCount} path(s)) ──`);
   247	    const rc = runGroup(g, subject, args);
   248	    worst = Math.max(worst, rc);
   249	  }
   250	  return worst;
   251	}
   252	
   253	// One bounded reviewer call for one group. The adapter does the signing; this
   254	// only narrows what it is pointed at, and records which group it was.
   255	function runGroup(group, subject, args) {
   256	  const a = [ADAPTERS, '--run',
   257	    '--reviewer', args.reviewer || 'codex',
   258	    '--packet', args.packet,
   259	    '--timeout', String(args.timeout || 420)];
   260	  if (args.base) a.push('--base', args.base);
   261	  if (args.head) a.push('--head', args.head);
   262	  for (const p of group.paths) a.push('--only-path', p);
   263	  a.push('--group-id', group.groupId, '--group-digest', group.groupDigest,
   264	         '--subject-sha', subject.subjectSha256);
   265	
   266	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: 'inherit', timeout: (Number(args.timeout || 420) + 120) * 1000 });
   267	  return r.status === null ? EXIT_REFUSED : r.status;
   268	}
   269	
   270	// ── aggregation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   271	function loadGroupRecords() {
   272	  if (!fs.existsSync(GROUPS_DIR)) return [];
   273	  return fs.readdirSync(GROUPS_DIR)
   274	    .filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'))
   275	    .sort()
   276	    .map((f) => {
   277	      const p = path.join(GROUPS_DIR, f);
   278	      try { return { path: p, rec: JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8')) }; }
   279	      catch (e) { return { path: p, rec: null, error: e.message }; }
   280	    });
   281	}
   282	
   283	function aggregate(args) {
   284	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   285	  const plan = planGroups(subject.subjectPaths, Number(args.groups) > 0 ? Number(args.groups) : DEFAULT_GROUPS);
   286	  const loaded = loadGroupRecords();
   287	  const problems = [];
   288	
   289	  const usable = [];
   290	  for (const l of loaded) {
   291	    if (!l.rec) { problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNREADABLE', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)}: ${l.error}` }); continue; }
   292	    // Every group record must be signed and verify. An unsigned group is not a
   293	    // smaller piece of evidence — it is no evidence.
   294	    const v = require('./review-sign.cjs').verify(l.rec, { packetPath: args.packet });
   295	    if (!v.ok) { problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNSIGNED', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)}: ${v.code} — ${v.reason}` }); continue; }
   296	    if (!l.rec.reviewOf || l.rec.reviewOf.diffSha256 !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   297	      problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-STALE-SUBJECT', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)} is bound to a different subject; a verdict on one revision never transfers to another` });
   298	      continue;
   299	    }
   300	    usable.push(l.rec);
   301	  }
   302	
   303	  // One reviewer, one model, across every group. Mixing them produces an
   304	  // aggregate nobody can attribute — "Codex approved it" would be true of some
   305	  // groups and false of others.
   306	  const reviewers = [...new Set(usable.map((r) => r.reviewer))];
   307	  const models = [...new Set(usable.map((r) => r.reviewerModel))];
   308	  if (reviewers.length > 1) problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-MIXED-REVIEWER', detail: `groups were reviewed by different reviewers: ${reviewers.join(', ')}` });
   309	  if (models.length > 1) problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-MIXED-MODEL', detail: `groups were reviewed by different models: ${models.join(', ')}` });
   310	
   311	  const unavailable = usable.filter((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   312	  if (unavailable.length) {
   313	    problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${unavailable.length} group(s) could not be reviewed: ${unavailable.map((r) => (r.group && r.group.groupId) || r.reviewId).join(', ')}. A timeout on one group leaves that code unreviewed; the aggregate cannot cover for it.` });
   314	  }
   315	
   316	  const cov = checkCoverage(
   317	    usable.map((r) => ({ groupId: (r.group && r.group.groupId) || r.reviewId, paths: (r.reviewOf && r.reviewOf.changedPaths) || [] })),
   318	    subject.subjectPaths
   319	  );
   320	  if (!cov.ok) problems.push({ code: cov.code, detail: cov.reason });
   321	
   322	  const ok = problems.length === 0 && usable.length > 0;
   323	  const findings = usable.flatMap((r) => r.findings || []);
   324	  const disposition = !ok ? 'UNAVAILABLE'
   325	    : usable.some((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT') ? 'REJECT'
   326	    : usable.some((r) => r.disposition === 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES') ? 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES'
   327	    : 'APPROVE';
   328	
   329	  return { ok, problems, usable, plan, subject, disposition, findings, cov };
   330	}
   331	
   332	function cmdAggregate(args) {
   333	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   334	  const a = aggregate(args);
   335	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   336	
   337	  const record = {
   338	    reviewId: `REV-${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-aggregate`,
   339	    ts,
   340	    reviewer: a.usable.length ? a.usable[0].reviewer : (args.reviewer || 'codex'),
   341	    reviewerModel: a.usable.length ? a.usable[0].reviewerModel : 'unknown',
   342	    packetId: (() => { try { return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8')).packetId; } catch { return 'unknown'; } })(),
   343	    reviewOf: { diffSha256: a.subject.subjectSha256, changedPaths: a.subject.subjectPaths.slice() },
   344	    disposition: a.disposition,
   345	    findings: a.ok ? a.findings : [],
   346	    aggregate: {
   347	      groupCount: a.usable.length,
   348	      plannedGroupCount: a.plan.length,
   349	      // Each group's own attestation digest is embedded. Editing a group record
   350	      // after aggregation changes its digest and this aggregate stops matching,
   351	      // so an aggregate cannot outlive the evidence it was built from.
   352	      groups: a.usable.map((r) => ({
   353	        groupId: (r.group && r.group.groupId) || null,
   354	        groupDigest: (r.group && r.group.groupDigest) || null,
   355	        pathCount: ((r.reviewOf && r.reviewOf.changedPaths) || []).length,
   356	        disposition: r.disposition,
   357	        reviewId: r.reviewId,
   358	        attestationDigest: (r.attestation && r.attestation.payloadDigest) || null,
   359	      })),
   360	      coverage: a.cov.ok ? 'EXACT' : a.cov.code,
   361	      problems: a.problems,
   362	    },
   363	  };
   364	  if (!a.ok) {
   365	    record.unavailableReason = `chunked review did not produce consumable evidence: ${a.problems.map((p) => p.code).join(', ')}`;
   366	  }
   367	
   368	  const signed = require('./review-sign.cjs').sign(record, { packetPath: args.packet });
   369	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   370	  const outPath = path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, `${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-aggregate.json`);
   371	  fs.writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(signed, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   372	
   373	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(signed, null, 2)); return a.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_REFUSED; }
   374	
   375	  console.log('AEGIS — AGGREGATE REVIEW VERDICT');
   376	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   377	  console.log(`subject     : ${a.subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}…`);
   378	  console.log(`groups      : ${a.usable.length} usable of ${a.plan.length} planned`);
   379	  console.log(`coverage    : ${a.cov.ok ? 'EXACT' : a.cov.code}`);
   380	  console.log(`disposition : ${a.disposition}`);
   381	  console.log(`findings    : ${record.findings.length}`);
   382	  console.log(`record      : ${path.relative(ROOT, outPath)}  (signed)`);
   383	  if (a.problems.length) {
   384	    console.log('');
   385	    console.log('NOT CONSUMABLE:');
   386	    for (const p of a.problems) { console.log(`  ${p.code}`); console.log(`    ${p.detail}`); }
   387	    console.log('');
   388	    console.log('The aggregate is written as UNAVAILABLE so the failure is recorded');
   389	    console.log('evidence rather than an absence. It will block the gate.');
   390	  }
   391	  return a.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_REFUSED;
   392	}
   393	
   394	function usage(msg) {
   395	  if (msg) process.stderr.write(`\n[review-chunker] ${msg}\n`);
   396	  process.stderr.write(`
   397	review-chunker.cjs — chunked review for subjects too large for one call
   398	
   399	  --plan [--groups N] [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--json]
   400	  --run --group <G1..Gn> --packet <p> [--reviewer codex|grok] [--timeout <s>]
   401	  --run-all --packet <p> [--reviewer codex|grok] [--timeout <s>]
   402	  --aggregate --packet <p> [--json]
   403	
   404	An aggregate is consumable only on EXACT coverage with every group signed.
   405	`);
   406	  return EXIT_USAGE;
   407	}
   408	
   409	function parseArgs(argv) {
   410	  const a = {};
   411	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   412	    const t = argv[i];
   413	    if (t === '--plan') a.plan = true;
   414	    else if (t === '--run') a.run = true;
   415	    else if (t === '--run-all') { a.run = true; a.all = true; }
   416	    else if (t === '--aggregate') a.aggregate = true;
   417	    else if (t === '--group') a.group = argv[++i];
   418	    else if (t === '--groups') a.groups = argv[++i];
   419	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   420	    else if (t === '--reviewer') a.reviewer = argv[++i];
   421	    else if (t === '--timeout') a.timeout = argv[++i];
   422	    else if (t === '--base') a.base = argv[++i];
   423	    else if (t === '--head') a.head = argv[++i];
   424	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   425	  }
   426	  return a;
   427	}
   428	
   429	if (require.main === module) {
   430	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   431	  let code;
   432	  try {
   433	    if (args.plan) code = cmdPlan(args);
   434	    else if (args.run) code = cmdRun(args);
   435	    else if (args.aggregate) code = cmdAggregate(args);
   436	    else code = usage();
   437	  } catch (e) {
   438	    process.stderr.write(`\n[review-chunker] ${e.message}\n`);
   439	    code = EXIT_REFUSED;
   440	  }
   441	  process.exit(code);
   442	}
   443	
   444	module.exports = { planGroups, checkCoverage, aggregate, roleOf, ROLES, DEFAULT_GROUPS };
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * review-adapters.cjs — read-only bridges from a reviewer tool to a review record.
     4	 *
     5	 * WHAT AN ADAPTER IS ALLOWED TO DO
     6	 * Run a reviewer against the bound subject diff, keep its raw output verbatim,
     7	 * and translate that output into a record conforming to
     8	 * schemas/engineering-review.schema.json.
     9	 *
    10	 * WHAT AN ADAPTER MAY NEVER DO
    11	 * Invent a verdict. Every failure path here — tool missing, tool crashed, tool
    12	 * timed out, output unparseable, output failed schema validation — produces
    13	 * disposition UNAVAILABLE with a concrete reason. There is no code path in this
    14	 * file that emits APPROVE without a reviewer having actually said so, and the
    15	 * gate treats UNAVAILABLE as a block, so a broken adapter stops the build
    16	 * rather than waving it through. That asymmetry is the entire design.
    17	 *
    18	 * READ-ONLY IS ENFORCED AT THE TOOL, NOT REQUESTED IN THE PROMPT
    19	 * Codex runs under `--sandbox read-only`. Grok runs with a single-turn `-p`
    20	 * prompt and no approval flags, so it has no path to an accepted write. Asking
    21	 * a model nicely not to edit files is not a control.
    22	 *
    23	 *   node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor [--json]
    24	 *   node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --run --reviewer codex|grok|copilot \
    25	 *        --subject-sha <sha> --packet <packet.json> [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] \
    26	 *        [--timeout <seconds>] [--dry-run]
    27	 *
    28	 * Exit: 0 record written (any disposition) · 2 usage · 3 could not produce a record
    29	 */
    30	'use strict';
    31	
    32	const fs = require('fs');
    33	const path = require('path');
    34	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    35	
    36	const HERE = __dirname;
    37	const ROOT = path.resolve(HERE, '..');
    38	const REVIEWS_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
    39	const RAW_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'review-raw');
    40	const ENGOS = path.join(HERE, 'engineering-os.cjs');
    41	
    42	const EXIT_PASS = 0;
    43	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    44	const EXIT_BLOCK = 3;
    45	
    46	// Absolute, verified install locations. These are recorded rather than
    47	// discovered on PATH because neither tool puts itself on PATH here, and a
    48	// `command -v codex` miss previously led to reporting a tool as absent when it
    49	// was installed all along.
    50	const TOOLS = {
    51	  codex: {
    52	    bin: '/Applications/ChatGPT.app/Contents/Resources/codex',
    53	    label: 'Codex CLI (ChatGPT.app)',
    54	    role: 'independent reviewer',
    55	  },
    56	  grok: {
    57	    bin: '/Users/marcpapineau/.grok/downloads/grok-macos-aarch64',
    58	    label: 'Grok CLI',
    59	    role: 'adversarial red team',
    60	  },
    61	};
    62	
    63	// ── detection ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    64	function isExecutable(p) {
    65	  try { fs.accessSync(p, fs.constants.X_OK); return true; } catch { return false; }
    66	}
    67	
    68	function detect() {
    69	  const result = {};
    70	  for (const [name, t] of Object.entries(TOOLS)) {
    71	    const present = fs.existsSync(t.bin);
    72	    const exec = present && isExecutable(t.bin);
    73	    result[name] = {
    74	      status: exec ? 'AVAILABLE' : 'UNAVAILABLE',
    75	      bin: t.bin,
    76	      label: t.label,
    77	      role: t.role,
    78	      detail: exec ? 'present and executable'
    79	        : present ? 'present but not executable'
    80	        : 'not found at the recorded absolute path',
    81	    };
    82	  }
    83	  // Copilot is not a local binary. Its review capability lives behind the
    84	  // GitHub API and a plan entitlement, so the only honest local check is
    85	  // whether `gh` exists and is authenticated — which is necessary, not
    86	  // sufficient. Whether the account can actually request a Copilot review is
    87	  // unproven until a real PR exists to request one on.
    88	  const gh = spawnSync('gh', ['auth', 'status'], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    89	  const ghOk = !gh.error && gh.status === 0;
    90	  result.copilot = {
    91	    status: 'UNVERIFIED',
    92	    bin: 'gh (GitHub CLI)',
    93	    label: 'GitHub Copilot code review',
    94	    role: 'repository guardian',
    95	    detail: ghOk
    96	      ? 'gh is installed and authenticated, which is necessary but NOT sufficient — Copilot review requires a plan entitlement that cannot be confirmed without requesting a review on a real PR'
    97	      : 'gh is missing or not authenticated; Copilot review cannot be requested or collected',
    98	  };
    99	  return result;
   100	}
   101	
   102	function cmdDoctor(args) {
   103	  const d = detect();
   104	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(d, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
   105	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEWER DOCTOR');
   106	  console.log('='.repeat(60));
   107	  for (const [name, r] of Object.entries(d)) {
   108	    console.log(`${r.status.padEnd(12)} ${name}  (${r.role})`);
   109	    console.log(`             ${r.bin}`);
   110	    console.log(`             ${r.detail}`);
   111	  }
   112	  console.log('');
   113	  console.log('AVAILABLE means the binary exists and can be executed. It does NOT');
   114	  console.log('mean a review has been run, authenticated, or paid for.');
   115	  return EXIT_PASS;
   116	}
   117	
   118	// ── subject resolution (delegates; does not re-implement) ───────────────────
   119	function subjectOf(args) {
   120	  const a = [ENGOS, '--subject', '--json'];
   121	  if (args.base) a.push('--base', args.base);
   122	  if (args.head) a.push('--head', args.head);
   123	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   124	  if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`could not compute subject: ${(r.stderr || '').trim()}`);
   125	  return JSON.parse(r.stdout);
   126	}
   127	
   128	function subjectDiff(subject, args) {
   129	  const a = ['diff'];
   130	  if (args.base) a.push(`${args.base}..${args.head || 'HEAD'}`);
   131	  else a.push(args.head || 'HEAD');
   132	  a.push('--', ...subject.subjectPaths);
   133	  const r = spawnSync('git', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 256 * 1024 * 1024 });
   134	  return r.status === 0 ? r.stdout : '';
   135	}
   136	
   137	// ── prompts ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   138	// Bounded on purpose: the requirement, the authoritative paths, the diff, and
   139	// the exact output contract. No conversation history, and — for Codex — no
   140	// account of what the builder believes. A reviewer handed the author's
   141	// justification reviews the justification.
   142	const RECORD_CONTRACT = `
   143	Return ONLY a JSON object, no prose around it, with exactly these fields:
   144	{
   145	  "disposition": "APPROVE" | "APPROVE_WITH_NOTES" | "REJECT",
   146	  "findings": [
   147	    {
   148	      "severity": "CRITICAL"|"HIGH"|"MEDIUM"|"LOW"|"INFORMATIONAL",
   149	      "file": "<repo-relative path>",
   150	      "location": "<line/range/symbol>",
   151	      "problem": "<what is wrong>",
   152	      "evidence": "<the code, output, or spec line that shows it — REQUIRED, non-empty>",
   153	      "impact": "<what breaks — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   154	      "requiredCorrection": "<what must change — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   155	      "verificationMethod": "<what proves the fix — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   156	      "status": "OPEN"
   157	    }
   158	  ],
   159	  "unverified": ["<anything you could not check>"]
   160	}
   161	Rules: a finding with no evidence is an opinion — omit it. Do not manufacture
   162	findings to justify the review; zero findings is a valid, useful result.`;
   163	
   164	// CONFIRMED FINDING #6: prompts carried only the objective, paths and diff.
   165	// The packet requires reviewers to receive authoritative paths, test evidence
   166	// and explicit unverified items — without them a reviewer cannot tell an
   167	// intentional design from an accident, and cannot know which claims were
   168	// actually checked. Bounded: digests and a tail, never whole files.
   169	function evidenceBlock(ctx) {
   170	  if (!ctx) return '';
   171	  const lines = [];
   172	  if (ctx.specs && ctx.specs.length) {
   173	    lines.push('', 'PINNED SPECIFICATIONS (authoritative intent, by content digest):');
   174	    for (const sp of ctx.specs) lines.push(`  ${sp.path}  sha256:${sp.sha.slice(0, 16)}…`);
   175	  }
   176	  if (ctx.checks && ctx.checks.length) {
   177	    lines.push('', 'DETERMINISTIC EVIDENCE (already executed against this subject):');
   178	    for (const c of ctx.checks) lines.push(`  exit ${c.exit}  ${c.cmd}`);
   179	    lines.push('', 'Treat a passing check as evidence that the command ran, NOT that the');
   180	    lines.push('requirement is met. If a test passes but a user would still see wrong');
   181	    lines.push('behaviour, that is exactly the finding worth reporting.');
   182	  }
   183	  if (ctx.unverified && ctx.unverified.length) {
   184	    lines.push('', 'EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED (nobody checked these — do not assume either way):');
   185	    for (const u of ctx.unverified) lines.push(`  - ${u}`);
   186	  }
   187	  return lines.join('\n');
   188	}
   189	
   190	function codexPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   191	  return `You are an independent senior technical lead reviewing a change.
   192	
   193	Do not assume the implementation is correct. Independently determine what
   194	correct behaviour should be from the stated objective, then inspect what was
   195	actually written.
   196	
   197	OBJECTIVE (from the approved task packet):
   198	${objective}
   199	
   200	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   201	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   202	
   203	Look for: architectural drift, incomplete functionality, regression, duplicated
   204	logic, state and concurrency errors, API/contract violations, auth mistakes,
   205	security problems, missing validation, edge cases, mock or hardcoded data,
   206	misleading tests, deleted functionality.
   207	
   208	${evidenceBlock(ctx)}
   209	
   210	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   211	${diff}
   212	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   213	}
   214	
   215	function grokPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   216	  return `You are the final adversarial reviewer. Assume this change contains a
   217	serious flaw that previous engineers missed, and try to prove it incorrect.
   218	
   219	Attack: requirement interpretation, edge cases, regression paths, authorization,
   220	security, data integrity, state, concurrency, error paths, dependency
   221	assumptions, and the tests themselves. Specifically hunt for cases where the
   222	tests PASS but a user still experiences wrong behaviour.
   223	
   224	Do not invent theoretical criticism without evidence.
   225	
   226	OBJECTIVE:
   227	${objective}
   228	
   229	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   230	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   231	
   232	${evidenceBlock(ctx)}
   233	
   234	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   235	${diff}
   236	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   237	}
   238	
   239	// ── raw output -> record ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   240	// Extract the first balanced JSON object. Models wrap JSON in prose and fences
   241	// no matter how firmly the contract says not to; that is a formatting quirk,
   242	// not a reason to discard a real review.
   243	function extractJson(text) {
   244	  if (typeof text !== 'string') return null;
   245	  const start = text.indexOf('{');
   246	  if (start === -1) return null;
   247	  let depth = 0, inStr = false, esc = false;
   248	  for (let i = start; i < text.length; i++) {
   249	    const c = text[i];
   250	    if (inStr) {
   251	      if (esc) esc = false;
   252	      else if (c === '\\') esc = true;
   253	      else if (c === '"') inStr = false;
   254	      continue;
   255	    }
   256	    if (c === '"') inStr = true;
   257	    else if (c === '{') depth++;
   258	    else if (c === '}') {
   259	      depth--;
   260	      if (depth === 0) {
   261	        try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(start, i + 1)); } catch { return null; }
   262	      }
   263	    }
   264	  }
   265	  return null;
   266	}
   267	
   268	// A parsed object is only a review if it actually carries a verdict. Some CLIs
   269	// wrap their run in a JSON envelope ({text, stopReason, usage…}); extracting
   270	// that envelope must not be mistaken for extracting a review.
   271	function isUsableReview(o) {
   272	  return !!(o && typeof o === 'object' && typeof o.disposition === 'string');
   273	}
   274	
   275	// Build a record. `parsed` null/unusable => UNAVAILABLE, never APPROVE.
   276	function buildRecord({ reviewer, reviewerModel, packetId, subject, parsed, unavailableReason, ts }) {
   277	  const base = {
   278	    reviewId: `REV-${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-${reviewer}`,
   279	    ts,
   280	    reviewer,
   281	    reviewerModel,
   282	    packetId,
   283	    reviewOf: {
   284	      diffSha256: subject.subjectSha256,
   285	      changedPaths: subject.subjectPaths.slice(),
   286	    },
   287	  };
   288	  if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || !parsed.disposition) {
   289	    return {
   290	      ...base,
   291	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   292	      unavailableReason: unavailableReason || 'the reviewer produced no parseable review record',
   293	      findings: [],
   294	    };
   295	  }
   296	  const allowed = ['APPROVE', 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES', 'REJECT'];
   297	  if (!allowed.includes(parsed.disposition)) {
   298	    return {
   299	      ...base,
   300	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   301	      unavailableReason: `the reviewer returned an unrecognised disposition ${JSON.stringify(parsed.disposition)}`,
   302	      findings: [],
   303	    };
   304	  }
   305	  const findings = Array.isArray(parsed.findings) ? parsed.findings : [];
   306	  return {
   307	    ...base,
   308	    disposition: parsed.disposition,
   309	    findings: findings
   310	      // A finding with empty evidence is dropped rather than passed through to
   311	      // fail schema validation and take the whole record down with it. Dropping
   312	      // an unevidenced finding is safe; dropping an evidenced one never happens.
   313	      .filter((f) => f && typeof f.evidence === 'string' && f.evidence.trim() !== '')
   314	      .map((f) => ({
   315	        severity: f.severity || 'INFORMATIONAL',
   316	        file: f.file || '(unspecified)',
   317	        location: f.location || '',
   318	        problem: f.problem || '',
   319	        evidence: f.evidence,
   320	        impact: f.impact || '',
   321	        requiredCorrection: f.requiredCorrection || '',
   322	        verificationMethod: f.verificationMethod || '',
   323	        status: 'OPEN',
   324	      })),
   325	    unverified: Array.isArray(parsed.unverified) ? parsed.unverified.map(String) : [],
   326	  };
   327	}
   328	
   329	function validateRecord(recordPath) {
   330	  const r = spawnSync('node', [ENGOS, '--validate-review', recordPath, '--json'],
   331	    { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   332	  let parsed = [];
   333	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* fall through */ }
   334	  const first = parsed[0] || { ok: false, errors: ['validator produced no result'] };
   335	  return { ok: !!first.ok, errors: first.errors || [] };
   336	}
   337	
   338	// ── run ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   339	// CONFIRMED FINDING #4: adapters selected hardcoded binaries directly and
   340	// ignored the Tool Capability Canon, so disabling a reviewer in the canon had
   341	// no effect on what actually ran, and dashboard/router state could disagree
   342	// with execution. The canon is now consulted before any tool is launched.
   343	// CONFIRMED FINDING #5: canonGate only rejected enabled=false / DISABLED, so a
   344	// tool whose availability was UNVERIFIED or BLOCKED still ran, and runTool()
   345	// spawned Grok directly without ever consulting routeRole() — meaning the
   346	// metered authorization, data-class veto and role separation in
   347	// MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json governed nothing at execution time. There is now
   348	// ONE authorization path and every launch goes through it.
   349	function authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts = {}) {
   350	  const canon = canonGate(reviewer);
   351	  if (!canon.ok) return canon;
   352	
   353	  // Availability must be positively AVAILABLE with evidence. UNVERIFIED is not
   354	  // a soft yes — it is the absence of a check.
   355	  const avail = canon.tool.availability;
   356	  if (avail !== 'AVAILABLE') {
   357	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is ${avail} in the Tool Capability Canon; only AVAILABLE (with evidence) may be launched` };
   358	  }
   359	  if (!canon.tool.availabilityEvidence || !canon.tool.availabilityEvidence.observedAt) {
   360	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is marked AVAILABLE but carries no dated availability evidence` };
   361	  }
   362	
   363	  // Role routing: data-class veto, metered authorization, role separation.
   364	  const roleId = reviewer === 'grok' ? 'adversarial-review'
   365	    : reviewer === 'codex' ? 'implementation-review'
   366	    : 'repository-guardian';
   367	  let route;
   368	  try {
   369	    route = require('./tool-router.cjs').routeRole(roleId, {
   370	      dataClass: opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL',
   371	      allowMetered: opts.allowMetered,
   372	      approvedBy: opts.approvedBy,
   373	      capUsd: opts.capUsd,
   374	    });
   375	  } catch (e) {
   376	    return { ok: false, reason: `model routing policy unusable: ${e.message}` };
   377	  }
   378	  if (!route.ok) return { ok: false, reason: `${route.code}: ${route.reason}` };
   379	  return { ok: true, tool: canon.tool, route };
   380	}
   381	
   382	function canonGate(reviewer) {
   383	  const fsx = require('fs');
   384	  const canonPath = require('path').join(HERE, 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json');
   385	  if (!fsx.existsSync(canonPath)) {
   386	    return { ok: false, reason: 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json is missing — refusing to run a reviewer the canon cannot authorize' };
   387	  }
   388	  let canon;
   389	  try { canon = JSON.parse(fsx.readFileSync(canonPath, 'utf8')); }
   390	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}` }; }
   391	  const wanted = { codex: 'codex-local', grok: 'grok-cli' }[reviewer];
   392	  const tool = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === wanted);
   393	  if (!tool) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is not declared in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   394	  if (tool.enabled === false) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is DISABLED in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   395	  if (tool.availability === 'DISABLED') return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is DISABLED in the canon` };
   396	  return { ok: true, tool };
   397	}
   398	
   399	// Build the exact argument vector a reviewer will be launched with. Exported
   400	// so a test can assert that every bound the policy DECLARES is actually
   401	// PRESENT on the command line — the gap that let maxTurns:1 run 13 turns.
   402	function buildToolArgv(reviewer, prompt, bounds) {
   403	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   404	    return ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   405	  }
   406	  const argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   407	  if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   408	  if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   409	  return argv;
   410	}
   411	
   412	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, opts = {}) {
   413	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   414	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   415	  const gateResult = authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts);
   416	  if (!gateResult.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gateResult.reason, raw: '' };
   417	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   418	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   419	  }
   420	  let argv;
   421	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   422	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   423	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   424	  } else {
   425	    // Bounds come from MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json, not from this file, so the
   426	    // Product Owner can tighten an adversarial reviewer without editing code.
   427	    // Single-turn print mode, no approval flags, no web, no sub-agents: the
   428	    // adversary gets exactly one pass at the diff and no way to spend the
   429	    // budget on a conversation with itself.
   430	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   431	    let bounds = null;
   432	    try {
   433	      bounds = (require('./tool-router.cjs').loadPolicy().roles['adversarial-review'] || {}).bounds || null;
   434	    } catch { /* absent policy is handled by the router's own refusal */ }
   435	    // CONFIRMED DEFECT (2026-08-24, caught by reading a real run's telemetry):
   436	    // the policy declared maxTurns:1 and this adapter never passed it. Grok ran
   437	    // 13 turns and returned no record. A bound that is declared in a policy but
   438	    // not passed to the tool is theatre — it reads as governance in the JSON and
   439	    // enforces nothing at runtime. Every bound below is now wired to a real flag
   440	    // or refuses, and buildToolArgv() is asserted against the policy by test.
   441	    if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) {
   442	      argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   443	    }
   444	    if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   445	    // Sub-agents are disabled by OMITTING --agents entirely. Passing an empty
   446	    // value was a bug: the CLI expects a map and rejected the array outright,
   447	    // which failed the launch at argument parsing. No definitions supplied is
   448	    // the correct way to express "no sub-agents".
   449	    if (bounds && bounds.subagents === true) {
   450	      throw new Error('policy enables sub-agents for adversarial-review; this adapter deliberately does not implement that path');
   451	    }
   452	  }
   453	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   454	    encoding: 'utf8',
   455	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   456	    maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
   457	    cwd: ROOT,
   458	  });
   459	  const raw = (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr ? `\n--- stderr ---\n${r.stderr}` : '');
   460	  if (r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') {
   461	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exceeded the ${timeoutSec}s timeout`, raw };
   462	  }
   463	  if (r.error) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} failed to start: ${r.error.message}`, raw };
   464	  if (r.status !== 0) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exited ${r.status}`, raw };
   465	  return { ok: true, raw };
   466	}
   467	
   468	function cmdRun(args) {
   469	  const reviewer = args.reviewer;
   470	  if (!reviewer) return usage('--reviewer is required');
   471	  if (!['codex', 'grok', 'copilot'].includes(reviewer)) return usage(`unknown reviewer ${reviewer}`);
   472	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   473	  if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) return usage(`packet not found: ${args.packet}`);
   474	
   475	  const packet = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8'));
   476	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   477	
   478	  // Chunked review: the reviewer sees only this group's paths, but the record
   479	  // stays bound to the FULL subject hash. That is what lets several group
   480	  // records aggregate into one verdict about one revision — and what stops a
   481	  // group verdict being reused against a different revision.
   482	  if (args.onlyPaths && args.onlyPaths.length) {
   483	    const inSubject = new Set(subject.subjectPaths);
   484	    const foreign = args.onlyPaths.filter((p) => !inSubject.has(p));
   485	    if (foreign.length) {
   486	      console.error(`[review-adapters] refusing: ${foreign.length} --only-path value(s) are not in the subject: ${foreign.slice(0, 4).join(', ')}`);
   487	      return EXIT_BLOCK;
   488	    }
   489	    subject.subjectPaths = args.onlyPaths.slice().sort();
   490	  }
   491	
   492	  if (args.subjectSha && args.subjectSha !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   493	    console.error(`[review-adapters] refusing to review: --subject-sha ${args.subjectSha.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the current subject ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…`);
   494	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   495	  }
   496	
   497	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   498	  fs.mkdirSync(RAW_DIR, { recursive: true });
   499	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   500	  const stamp = ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14);
   501	
   502	  // Copilot has no local binary and no read-only path that avoids mutating a
   503	  // PR. During this pass GitHub mutation is forbidden outright, so the only
   504	  // honest output is UNAVAILABLE with the reason stated.
   505	  if (reviewer === 'copilot') {
   506	    const d = detect().copilot;
   507	    const record = buildRecord({
   508	      reviewer: 'copilot',
   509	      reviewerModel: 'github-copilot-code-review',
   510	      packetId: packet.packetId,
   511	      subject,
   512	      parsed: null,
   513	      unavailableReason:
   514	        `Copilot review could not be collected. ${d.detail}. Requesting a review requires an open pull request and a GitHub API mutation, which this correction pass forbids.`,
   515	      ts,
   516	    });
   517	    return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-copilot`, '(no tool invoked — Copilot requires an open PR)', { packetPath: args.packet });
   518	  }
   519	
   520	  const diff = subjectDiff(subject, args);
   521	  const prompt = reviewer === 'codex'
   522	    ? codexPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff)
   523	    : grokPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff);
   524	
   525	  if (args.dryRun) {
   526	    console.log(`[review-adapters] DRY RUN — ${reviewer}: would send ${prompt.length} chars over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} subject path(s). No tool invoked.`);
   527	    return EXIT_PASS;
   528	  }
   529	
   530	  const timeoutSec = Number(args.timeout) > 0 ? Number(args.timeout) : 900;
   531	  const res = runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, {
   532	    dataClass: args.dataClass || 'INTERNAL',
   533	    allowMetered: args.allowMetered,
   534	    approvedBy: args.approvedBy,
   535	    capUsd: args.capUsd,
   536	  });
   537	  const rawPath = path.join(RAW_DIR, `${stamp}-${reviewer}.txt`);
   538	  fs.writeFileSync(rawPath, res.raw || '(no output captured)', 'utf8');
   539	
   540	  const parsed = res.ok ? extractJson(res.raw) : null;
   541	  // When a tool exits 0 but returns no usable record, "no parseable JSON" is
   542	  // true but unhelpfully vague — and a vague UNAVAILABLE reason is the kind of
   543	  // thing an operator learns to ignore. Several CLIs report why they stopped
   544	  // (cancelled, max turns, context limit); surface that instead when present,
   545	  // because "cancelled after 9 turns" and "returned prose" call for completely
   546	  // different responses.
   547	  let why = res.ok ? 'the reviewer ran but produced no parseable JSON review record' : res.reason;
   548	  if (res.ok && !isUsableReview(parsed)) {
   549	    const envelope = extractJson(res.raw);
   550	    const stop = envelope && typeof envelope.stopReason === 'string' ? envelope.stopReason : null;
   551	    const turns = envelope && typeof envelope.num_turns === 'number' ? envelope.num_turns : null;
   552	    if (stop && stop !== 'end_turn') {
   553	      why = `the reviewer stopped early (stopReason="${stop}"${turns ? `, ${turns} turns` : ''}) without emitting a review record`;
   554	    }
   555	  }
   556	  const record = buildRecord({
   557	    reviewer,
   558	    reviewerModel: reviewer === 'codex' ? 'codex-cli (ChatGPT.app)' : 'grok-cli (grok-macos-aarch64)',
   559	    packetId: packet.packetId,
   560	    subject,
   561	    parsed: isUsableReview(parsed) ? parsed : null,
   562	    unavailableReason: why,
   563	    ts,
   564	  });
   565	  if (args.groupId) {
   566	    record.group = { groupId: args.groupId, groupDigest: args.groupDigest || null };
   567	  }
   568	  // Group records go into reviews/groups/, which the gate does NOT read. Only
   569	  // the aggregate reaches the gate, and only when every group beneath it
   570	  // covered the subject exactly. A stray group record must never be mistaken
   571	  // for a review of the whole change.
   572	  return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-${reviewer}${args.groupId ? '-' + args.groupId : ''}`, rawPath,
   573	    { packetPath: args.packet, subdir: args.groupId ? 'groups' : null });
   574	}
   575	
   576	function writeRecord(record, base, rawPath, opts = {}) {
   577	  const dir = opts.subdir ? path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, opts.subdir) : REVIEWS_DIR;
   578	  fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
   579	  const outPath = path.join(dir, `${base}.json`);
   580	  // ROOT CAUSE of the 2026-08-25 report: the adapter wrote records UNSIGNED, so
   581	  // every one it produced — including a timeout's UNAVAILABLE — landed in
   582	  // reviews/ as invalid active evidence. The gate then reported
   583	  // ENGOS-REVIEW-MALFORMED instead of the true state ("the reviewer timed out"),
   584	  // which is a worse diagnosis than the thing that actually happened.
   585	  //
   586	  // An UNAVAILABLE is attested exactly like any other outcome. "The reviewer
   587	  // could not run" is a real, useful, gateable fact and deserves the same
   588	  // integrity guarantee as an approval — otherwise the only records that carry
   589	  // provenance are the convenient ones.
   590	  let signed;
   591	  try {
   592	    signed = require('./review-sign.cjs').sign(record, { packetPath: opts.packetPath });
   593	  } catch (e) {
   594	    process.stderr.write(`[review-adapters] REFUSING to write an unsigned record: ${e.message}\n`);
   595	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   596	  }
   597	  fs.writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(signed, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   598	  record = signed;
   599	  const v = validateRecord(outPath);
   600	  console.log(`[review-adapters] reviewer   : ${record.reviewer} (${record.reviewerModel})`);
   601	  console.log(`[review-adapters] disposition: ${record.disposition}`);
   602	  if (record.unavailableReason) console.log(`[review-adapters] reason     : ${record.unavailableReason}`);
   603	  console.log(`[review-adapters] findings   : ${(record.findings || []).length}`);
   604	  console.log(`[review-adapters] raw output : ${rawPath}`);
   605	  console.log(`[review-adapters] record     : ${path.relative(ROOT, outPath)}`);
   606	  if (!v.ok) {
   607	    console.error('[review-adapters] the produced record FAILED schema validation:');
   608	    for (const e of v.errors) console.error(`    ${e}`);
   609	    console.error('[review-adapters] the record is left on disk for inspection. The gate will refuse it.');
   610	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   611	  }
   612	  console.log('[review-adapters] record is SIGNED and validates against engineering-review.schema.json');
   613	  return EXIT_PASS;
   614	}
   615	
   616	function usage(msg) {
   617	  if (msg) process.stderr.write(`\n[review-adapters] ${msg}\n`);
   618	  process.stderr.write(`
   619	review-adapters.cjs — read-only reviewer bridges
   620	
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * review-sign.cjs — attestation for review records.
     4	 *
     5	 * THE HOLE THIS CLOSES
     6	 * Before this, `builder-control/reviews/*.json` was plain JSON that the gate
     7	 * trusted. Anyone — including the builder — could write `{"reviewer":"codex",
     8	 * "disposition":"APPROVE"}` into that directory and the gate would count it as
     9	 * independent review. The whole "a builder may not approve its own work" rule
    10	 * was enforced by nothing but the builder's good manners.
    11	 *
    12	 * An attestation now covers the fields that make a record mean something:
    13	 *   subjectSha256 · packetDigest · reviewer · reviewerModel · disposition ·
    14	 *   a canonical digest of the findings
    15	 * Change any of them and the attestation stops verifying.
    16	 *
    17	 * WHAT THIS PROVES — AND WHAT IT DOES NOT
    18	 * This is an HMAC with a machine-local key. It proves a record was produced by
    19	 * this machine's adapter and has not been edited since. It does NOT prove a
    20	 * human approved anything, and it does NOT stop a process that can read the key
    21	 * from minting records. A local key cannot defend against local code; only a
    22	 * reviewer-held key or a remote attestation service could, and neither exists
    23	 * here yet.
    24	 *
    25	 * So the honest claim is narrow: hand-editing a verdict, copying a record
    26	 * between subjects, or dropping a fabricated APPROVE into reviews/ are now
    27	 * detected. Compromise of this machine is not. That limit is stated here rather
    28	 * than left for someone to discover, because an over-claimed control is worse
    29	 * than a missing one — it stops people looking.
    30	 *
    31	 *   node builder-control/review-sign.cjs --sign <record.json>
    32	 *   node builder-control/review-sign.cjs --verify <record.json>
    33	 *   node builder-control/review-sign.cjs --keyinfo
    34	 *
    35	 * Exit: 0 ok · 2 usage · 3 verification failed
    36	 */
    37	'use strict';
    38	
    39	const fs = require('fs');
    40	const path = require('path');
    41	const crypto = require('crypto');
    42	
    43	const HERE = __dirname;
    44	const ROOT = path.resolve(HERE, '..');
    45	const KEY_FILE = path.join(HERE, '.attestation-key');
    46	const PACKETS_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'packets');
    47	
    48	const EXIT_PASS = 0;
    49	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    50	const EXIT_FAIL = 3;
    51	
    52	const ALG = 'sha256';
    53	const VERSION = 'aegis-attest-v1';
    54	
    55	/**
    56	 * The key never enters the repository. It is read from the environment first
    57	 * (so CI can inject one), then from a gitignored file, and is generated with
    58	 * 0600 permissions if neither exists.
    59	 */
    60	function loadKey({ create = true } = {}) {
    61	  if (process.env.AEGIS_ATTESTATION_KEY) {
    62	    const k = process.env.AEGIS_ATTESTATION_KEY;
    63	    if (k.length < 32) throw new Error('AEGIS_ATTESTATION_KEY is shorter than 32 characters');
    64	    return { key: k, source: 'env:AEGIS_ATTESTATION_KEY' };
    65	  }
    66	  if (fs.existsSync(KEY_FILE)) {
    67	    const k = fs.readFileSync(KEY_FILE, 'utf8').trim();
    68	    if (k.length < 32) throw new Error(`${path.relative(ROOT, KEY_FILE)} is shorter than 32 characters`);
    69	    return { key: k, source: path.relative(ROOT, KEY_FILE) };
    70	  }
    71	  if (!create) throw new Error('no attestation key available');
    72	  const k = crypto.randomBytes(48).toString('base64url');
    73	  fs.writeFileSync(KEY_FILE, k + '\n', { mode: 0o600 });
    74	  try { fs.chmodSync(KEY_FILE, 0o600); } catch { /* best effort */ }
    75	  return { key: k, source: path.relative(ROOT, KEY_FILE), generated: true };
    76	}
    77	
    78	/** Stable digest of an arbitrary value — key order must not change the result. */
    79	function canonical(v) {
    80	  if (v === null || typeof v !== 'object') return JSON.stringify(v === undefined ? null : v);
    81	  if (Array.isArray(v)) return '[' + v.map(canonical).join(',') + ']';
    82	  return '{' + Object.keys(v).sort().map((k) => JSON.stringify(k) + ':' + canonical(v[k])).join(',') + '}';
    83	}
    84	const digest = (s) => crypto.createHash(ALG).update(s).digest('hex');
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * Digest of the packet's AUTHORIZING content. Finding #3: a review bound only
    88	 * to a packetId is not bound to what that packet permitted — the packet can be
    89	 * edited afterwards (and packets are excluded from the subject hash, so such an
    90	 * edit moves nothing). Hashing the authorization-bearing fields means widening
    91	 * filesAllowed after a review invalidates that review.
    92	 */
    93	function packetDigest(packetPath) {
    94	  if (!packetPath || !fs.existsSync(packetPath)) return null;
    95	  const p = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(packetPath, 'utf8'));
    96	  return digest(canonical({
    97	    packetId: p.packetId,
    98	    agentId: p.agentId,
    99	    objective: p.objective,
   100	    filesAllowed: p.filesAllowed,
   101	    authorization: p.authorization,
   102	    sourceOfTruth: p.sourceOfTruth,
   103	  }));
   104	}
   105	
   106	function resolvePacketForRecord(rec) {
   107	  if (!fs.existsSync(PACKETS_DIR)) return null;
   108	  for (const f of fs.readdirSync(PACKETS_DIR)) {
   109	    if (!f.endsWith('.json')) continue;
   110	    const full = path.join(PACKETS_DIR, f);
   111	    try {
   112	      if (JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(full, 'utf8')).packetId === rec.packetId) return full;
   113	    } catch { /* skip unreadable */ }
   114	  }
   115	  return null;
   116	}
   117	
   118	/** The exact bytes an attestation covers. */
   119	function attestationPayload(rec, pktDigest) {
   120	  return canonical({
   121	    v: VERSION,
   122	    subjectSha256: rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.diffSha256,
   123	    changedPaths: (rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.changedPaths) || [],
   124	    packetId: rec.packetId,
   125	    packetDigest: pktDigest,
   126	    reviewer: rec.reviewer,
   127	    reviewerModel: rec.reviewerModel,
   128	    disposition: rec.disposition,
   129	    // Findings are covered so severities cannot be downgraded after the fact.
   130	    findings: (rec.findings || []).map((f) => ({
   131	      severity: f.severity, file: f.file, problem: f.problem,
   132	      evidence: f.evidence, status: f.status,
   133	    })),
   134	  });
   135	}
   136	
   137	function sign(rec, opts = {}) {
   138	  const { key, source } = loadKey();
   139	  const pkt = opts.packetPath || resolvePacketForRecord(rec);
   140	  const pd = packetDigest(pkt);
   141	  const payload = attestationPayload(rec, pd);
   142	  const mac = crypto.createHmac(ALG, key).update(payload).digest('hex');
   143	  return {
   144	    ...rec,
   145	    attestation: {
   146	      v: VERSION, alg: `HMAC-${ALG.toUpperCase()}`,
   147	      packetDigest: pd,
   148	      payloadDigest: digest(payload),
   149	      mac,
   150	      signedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
   151	      keySource: source,
   152	      scope: 'Proves this record was produced by this machine\'s adapter and is unmodified. Does NOT prove human approval and does not defend against a process that can read the key.',
   153	    },
   154	  };
   155	}
   156	
   157	function verify(rec, opts = {}) {
   158	  if (!rec || !rec.attestation) {
   159	    return { ok: false, code: 'ATTESTATION-MISSING', reason: 'the record carries no attestation. An unsigned record is not review evidence — anyone can write JSON into reviews/.' };
   160	  }
   161	  const a = rec.attestation;
   162	  if (a.v !== VERSION) return { ok: false, code: 'ATTESTATION-VERSION', reason: `unknown attestation version ${a.v}` };
   163	
   164	  let key;
   165	  try { key = loadKey({ create: false }).key; }
   166	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, code: 'ATTESTATION-NO-KEY', reason: `cannot verify: ${e.message}. Refusing to accept an unverifiable record.` }; }
   167	
   168	  const pkt = opts.packetPath || resolvePacketForRecord(rec);
   169	  const pd = packetDigest(pkt);
   170	
   171	  // Finding #3: if the packet's authorizing content changed since signing, the
   172	  // review no longer describes the permissions it was granted under.
   173	  if (a.packetDigest && pd && a.packetDigest !== pd) {
   174	    return {
   175	      ok: false, code: 'ATTESTATION-PACKET-CHANGED',
   176	      reason: 'the task packet\'s authorization content changed after this review was signed. The review no longer describes what the packet permits.',
   177	    };
   178	  }
   179	
   180	  const payload = attestationPayload(rec, a.packetDigest);
   181	  const expect = crypto.createHmac(ALG, key).update(payload).digest('hex');
   182	  const got = String(a.mac || '');
   183	  const eq = got.length === expect.length &&
   184	    crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(got, 'hex'), Buffer.from(expect, 'hex'));
   185	  if (!eq) {
   186	    return {
   187	      ok: false, code: 'ATTESTATION-INVALID',
   188	      reason: 'the attestation does not match the record contents. A covered field (subject, packet, reviewer, model, disposition, or findings) was changed after signing.',
   189	    };
   190	  }
   191	  return { ok: true, packetDigest: pd };
   192	}
   193	
   194	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   195	if (require.main === module) {
   196	  const argv = process.argv.slice(2);
   197	  const file = argv[1];
   198	  let code = EXIT_PASS;
   199	  try {
   200	    if (argv[0] === '--keyinfo') {
   201	      const k = loadKey({ create: false });
   202	      console.log(`attestation key source: ${k.source}`);
   203	      console.log('the key itself is never printed and is never committed');
   204	    } else if (argv[0] === '--sign' && file) {
   205	      const rec = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
   206	      fs.writeFileSync(file, JSON.stringify(sign(rec), null, 2) + '\n');
   207	      console.log(`signed ${path.relative(ROOT, file)}`);
   208	    } else if (argv[0] === '--verify' && file) {
   209	      const r = verify(JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8')));
   210	      console.log(r.ok ? `VERIFIED  ${path.relative(ROOT, file)}` : `REFUSED   ${path.relative(ROOT, file)}\n  ${r.code}: ${r.reason}`);
   211	      code = r.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_FAIL;
   212	    } else {
   213	      process.stderr.write('usage: review-sign.cjs --sign|--verify <record.json> | --keyinfo\n');
   214	      code = EXIT_USAGE;
   215	    }
   216	  } catch (e) {
   217	    process.stderr.write(`\n[review-sign] ${e.message}\n`);
   218	    code = EXIT_FAIL;
   219	  }
   220	  process.exit(code);
   221	}
   222	
   223	module.exports = { sign, verify, packetDigest, attestationPayload, canonical, loadKey, resolvePacketForRecord, VERSION };
