   270	// ── aggregation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   271	function loadGroupRecords() {
   272	  if (!fs.existsSync(GROUPS_DIR)) return [];
   273	  return fs.readdirSync(GROUPS_DIR)
   274	    .filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'))
   275	    .sort()
   276	    .map((f) => {
   277	      const p = path.join(GROUPS_DIR, f);
   278	      try { return { path: p, rec: JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8')) }; }
   279	      catch (e) { return { path: p, rec: null, error: e.message }; }
   280	    });
   281	}
   282	
   283	function aggregate(args) {
   284	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   285	  const plan = planGroups(subject.subjectPaths, Number(args.groups) > 0 ? Number(args.groups) : DEFAULT_GROUPS);
   286	  const loaded = loadGroupRecords();
   287	  const problems = [];
   288	
   289	  const usable = [];
   290	  for (const l of loaded) {
   291	    if (!l.rec) { problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNREADABLE', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)}: ${l.error}` }); continue; }
   292	    // Every group record must be signed and verify. An unsigned group is not a
   293	    // smaller piece of evidence — it is no evidence.
   294	    const v = require('./review-sign.cjs').verify(l.rec, { packetPath: args.packet });
   295	    if (!v.ok) { problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNSIGNED', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)}: ${v.code} — ${v.reason}` }); continue; }
   296	    if (!l.rec.reviewOf || l.rec.reviewOf.diffSha256 !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   297	      problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-STALE-SUBJECT', detail: `${path.basename(l.path)} is bound to a different subject; a verdict on one revision never transfers to another` });
   298	      continue;
   299	    }
   300	    usable.push(l.rec);
   301	  }
   302	
   303	  // One reviewer, one model, across every group. Mixing them produces an
   304	  // aggregate nobody can attribute — "Codex approved it" would be true of some
   305	  // groups and false of others.
   306	  const reviewers = [...new Set(usable.map((r) => r.reviewer))];
   307	  const models = [...new Set(usable.map((r) => r.reviewerModel))];
   308	  if (reviewers.length > 1) problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-MIXED-REVIEWER', detail: `groups were reviewed by different reviewers: ${reviewers.join(', ')}` });
   309	  if (models.length > 1) problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-MIXED-MODEL', detail: `groups were reviewed by different models: ${models.join(', ')}` });
   310	
   311	  const unavailable = usable.filter((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   312	  if (unavailable.length) {
   313	    problems.push({ code: 'GROUP-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${unavailable.length} group(s) could not be reviewed: ${unavailable.map((r) => (r.group && r.group.groupId) || r.reviewId).join(', ')}. A timeout on one group leaves that code unreviewed; the aggregate cannot cover for it.` });
   314	  }
   315	
   316	  const cov = checkCoverage(
   317	    usable.map((r) => ({ groupId: (r.group && r.group.groupId) || r.reviewId, paths: (r.reviewOf && r.reviewOf.changedPaths) || [] })),
   318	    subject.subjectPaths
   319	  );
   320	  if (!cov.ok) problems.push({ code: cov.code, detail: cov.reason });
   321	
   322	  const ok = problems.length === 0 && usable.length > 0;
   323	  const findings = usable.flatMap((r) => r.findings || []);
   324	  const disposition = !ok ? 'UNAVAILABLE'
   325	    : usable.some((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT') ? 'REJECT'
   326	    : usable.some((r) => r.disposition === 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES') ? 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES'
   327	    : 'APPROVE';
   328	
   329	  return { ok, problems, usable, plan, subject, disposition, findings, cov };
   330	}
   331	
   332	function cmdAggregate(args) {
   333	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   334	  const a = aggregate(args);
   335	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   336	
   337	  const record = {
   338	    reviewId: `REV-${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-aggregate`,
   339	    ts,
   340	    reviewer: a.usable.length ? a.usable[0].reviewer : (args.reviewer || 'codex'),
   341	    reviewerModel: a.usable.length ? a.usable[0].reviewerModel : 'unknown',
   342	    packetId: (() => { try { return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8')).packetId; } catch { return 'unknown'; } })(),
   343	    reviewOf: { diffSha256: a.subject.subjectSha256, changedPaths: a.subject.subjectPaths.slice() },
   344	    disposition: a.disposition,
   345	    findings: a.ok ? a.findings : [],
   346	    aggregate: {
   347	      groupCount: a.usable.length,
   348	      plannedGroupCount: a.plan.length,
   349	      // Each group's own attestation digest is embedded. Editing a group record
   350	      // after aggregation changes its digest and this aggregate stops matching,
   351	      // so an aggregate cannot outlive the evidence it was built from.
   352	      groups: a.usable.map((r) => ({
   353	        groupId: (r.group && r.group.groupId) || null,
   354	        groupDigest: (r.group && r.group.groupDigest) || null,
   355	        pathCount: ((r.reviewOf && r.reviewOf.changedPaths) || []).length,
   356	        disposition: r.disposition,
   357	        reviewId: r.reviewId,
   358	        attestationDigest: (r.attestation && r.attestation.payloadDigest) || null,
   359	      })),
   360	      coverage: a.cov.ok ? 'EXACT' : a.cov.code,
   361	      problems: a.problems,
   362	    },
   363	  };
   364	  if (!a.ok) {
   365	    record.unavailableReason = `chunked review did not produce consumable evidence: ${a.problems.map((p) => p.code).join(', ')}`;
   366	  }
   367	
   368	  const signed = require('./review-sign.cjs').sign(record, { packetPath: args.packet });
   369	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   370	  const outPath = path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, `${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-aggregate.json`);
   371	  fs.writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(signed, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   372	
   373	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(signed, null, 2)); return a.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_REFUSED; }
   374	
   375	  console.log('AEGIS — AGGREGATE REVIEW VERDICT');
   376	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   377	  console.log(`subject     : ${a.subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}…`);
   378	  console.log(`groups      : ${a.usable.length} usable of ${a.plan.length} planned`);
   379	  console.log(`coverage    : ${a.cov.ok ? 'EXACT' : a.cov.code}`);
   380	  console.log(`disposition : ${a.disposition}`);
   381	  console.log(`findings    : ${record.findings.length}`);
   382	  console.log(`record      : ${path.relative(ROOT, outPath)}  (signed)`);
   383	  if (a.problems.length) {
   384	    console.log('');
   385	    console.log('NOT CONSUMABLE:');
   386	    for (const p of a.problems) { console.log(`  ${p.code}`); console.log(`    ${p.detail}`); }
   387	    console.log('');
   388	    console.log('The aggregate is written as UNAVAILABLE so the failure is recorded');
   389	    console.log('evidence rather than an absence. It will block the gate.');
   390	  }
   391	  return a.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_REFUSED;
   392	}
   393	
   394	function usage(msg) {
   395	  if (msg) process.stderr.write(`\n[review-chunker] ${msg}\n`);
   396	  process.stderr.write(`
   397	review-chunker.cjs — chunked review for subjects too large for one call
   398	
   399	  --plan [--groups N] [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--json]
   400	  --run --group <G1..Gn> --packet <p> [--reviewer codex|grok] [--timeout <s>]
   401	  --run-all --packet <p> [--reviewer codex|grok] [--timeout <s>]
   402	  --aggregate --packet <p> [--json]
   403	
   404	An aggregate is consumable only on EXACT coverage with every group signed.
   405	`);
   406	  return EXIT_USAGE;
   407	}
   408	
   409	function parseArgs(argv) {
   410	  const a = {};
   411	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   412	    const t = argv[i];
   413	    if (t === '--plan') a.plan = true;
   414	    else if (t === '--run') a.run = true;
   415	    else if (t === '--run-all') { a.run = true; a.all = true; }
   416	    else if (t === '--aggregate') a.aggregate = true;
   417	    else if (t === '--group') a.group = argv[++i];
   418	    else if (t === '--groups') a.groups = argv[++i];
   419	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   420	    else if (t === '--reviewer') a.reviewer = argv[++i];
   421	    else if (t === '--timeout') a.timeout = argv[++i];
   422	    else if (t === '--base') a.base = argv[++i];
   423	    else if (t === '--head') a.head = argv[++i];
   424	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   425	  }
   426	  return a;
   427	}
   428	
   429	if (require.main === module) {
   430	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   431	  let code;
   432	  try {
   433	    if (args.plan) code = cmdPlan(args);
   434	    else if (args.run) code = cmdRun(args);
   435	    else if (args.aggregate) code = cmdAggregate(args);
   436	    else code = usage();
   437	  } catch (e) {
   438	    process.stderr.write(`\n[review-chunker] ${e.message}\n`);
   439	    code = EXIT_REFUSED;
   440	  }
   441	  process.exit(code);
   442	}
   443	
   444	module.exports = { planGroups, checkCoverage, aggregate, roleOf, ROLES, DEFAULT_GROUPS };
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * review-adapters.cjs — read-only bridges from a reviewer tool to a review record.
     4	 *
     5	 * WHAT AN ADAPTER IS ALLOWED TO DO
     6	 * Run a reviewer against the bound subject diff, keep its raw output verbatim,
     7	 * and translate that output into a record conforming to
     8	 * schemas/engineering-review.schema.json.
     9	 *
    10	 * WHAT AN ADAPTER MAY NEVER DO
    11	 * Invent a verdict. Every failure path here — tool missing, tool crashed, tool
    12	 * timed out, output unparseable, output failed schema validation — produces
    13	 * disposition UNAVAILABLE with a concrete reason. There is no code path in this
    14	 * file that emits APPROVE without a reviewer having actually said so, and the
    15	 * gate treats UNAVAILABLE as a block, so a broken adapter stops the build
    16	 * rather than waving it through. That asymmetry is the entire design.
    17	 *
    18	 * READ-ONLY IS ENFORCED AT THE TOOL, NOT REQUESTED IN THE PROMPT
    19	 * Codex runs under `--sandbox read-only`. Grok runs with a single-turn `-p`
    20	 * prompt and no approval flags, so it has no path to an accepted write. Asking
    21	 * a model nicely not to edit files is not a control.
    22	 *
    23	 *   node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor [--json]
    24	 *   node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --run --reviewer codex|grok|copilot \
    25	 *        --subject-sha <sha> --packet <packet.json> [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] \
    26	 *        [--timeout <seconds>] [--dry-run]
    27	 *
    28	 * Exit: 0 record written (any disposition) · 2 usage · 3 could not produce a record
    29	 */
    30	'use strict';
    31	
    32	const fs = require('fs');
    33	const path = require('path');
    34	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    35	
    36	const HERE = __dirname;
    37	const ROOT = path.resolve(HERE, '..');
    38	const REVIEWS_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
    39	const RAW_DIR = path.join(HERE, 'review-raw');
    40	const ENGOS = path.join(HERE, 'engineering-os.cjs');
    41	
    42	const EXIT_PASS = 0;
    43	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    44	const EXIT_BLOCK = 3;
    45	
    46	// Absolute, verified install locations. These are recorded rather than
    47	// discovered on PATH because neither tool puts itself on PATH here, and a
    48	// `command -v codex` miss previously led to reporting a tool as absent when it
    49	// was installed all along.
    50	const TOOLS = {
    51	  codex: {
    52	    bin: '/Applications/ChatGPT.app/Contents/Resources/codex',
    53	    label: 'Codex CLI (ChatGPT.app)',
    54	    role: 'independent reviewer',
    55	  },
    56	  grok: {
    57	    bin: '/Users/marcpapineau/.grok/downloads/grok-macos-aarch64',
    58	    label: 'Grok CLI',
    59	    role: 'adversarial red team',
    60	  },
    61	};
    62	
    63	// ── detection ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    64	function isExecutable(p) {
    65	  try { fs.accessSync(p, fs.constants.X_OK); return true; } catch { return false; }
    66	}
    67	
    68	function detect() {
    69	  const result = {};
    70	  for (const [name, t] of Object.entries(TOOLS)) {
    71	    const present = fs.existsSync(t.bin);
    72	    const exec = present && isExecutable(t.bin);
    73	    result[name] = {
    74	      status: exec ? 'AVAILABLE' : 'UNAVAILABLE',
    75	      bin: t.bin,
    76	      label: t.label,
    77	      role: t.role,
    78	      detail: exec ? 'present and executable'
    79	        : present ? 'present but not executable'
    80	        : 'not found at the recorded absolute path',
    81	    };
    82	  }
    83	  // Copilot is not a local binary. Its review capability lives behind the
    84	  // GitHub API and a plan entitlement, so the only honest local check is
    85	  // whether `gh` exists and is authenticated — which is necessary, not
    86	  // sufficient. Whether the account can actually request a Copilot review is
    87	  // unproven until a real PR exists to request one on.
    88	  const gh = spawnSync('gh', ['auth', 'status'], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    89	  const ghOk = !gh.error && gh.status === 0;
    90	  result.copilot = {
    91	    status: 'UNVERIFIED',
    92	    bin: 'gh (GitHub CLI)',
    93	    label: 'GitHub Copilot code review',
    94	    role: 'repository guardian',
    95	    detail: ghOk
    96	      ? 'gh is installed and authenticated, which is necessary but NOT sufficient — Copilot review requires a plan entitlement that cannot be confirmed without requesting a review on a real PR'
    97	      : 'gh is missing or not authenticated; Copilot review cannot be requested or collected',
    98	  };
    99	  return result;
   100	}
   101	
   102	function cmdDoctor(args) {
   103	  const d = detect();
   104	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(d, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
   105	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEWER DOCTOR');
   106	  console.log('='.repeat(60));
   107	  for (const [name, r] of Object.entries(d)) {
   108	    console.log(`${r.status.padEnd(12)} ${name}  (${r.role})`);
   109	    console.log(`             ${r.bin}`);
   110	    console.log(`             ${r.detail}`);
   111	  }
   112	  console.log('');
   113	  console.log('AVAILABLE means the binary exists and can be executed. It does NOT');
   114	  console.log('mean a review has been run, authenticated, or paid for.');
   115	  return EXIT_PASS;
   116	}
   117	
   118	// ── subject resolution (delegates; does not re-implement) ───────────────────
   119	function subjectOf(args) {
   120	  const a = [ENGOS, '--subject', '--json'];
   121	  if (args.base) a.push('--base', args.base);
   122	  if (args.head) a.push('--head', args.head);
   123	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   124	  if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`could not compute subject: ${(r.stderr || '').trim()}`);
   125	  return JSON.parse(r.stdout);
   126	}
   127	
   128	function subjectDiff(subject, args) {
   129	  const a = ['diff'];
   130	  if (args.base) a.push(`${args.base}..${args.head || 'HEAD'}`);
   131	  else a.push(args.head || 'HEAD');
   132	  a.push('--', ...subject.subjectPaths);
   133	  const r = spawnSync('git', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 256 * 1024 * 1024 });
   134	  return r.status === 0 ? r.stdout : '';
   135	}
   136	
   137	// ── prompts ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   138	// Bounded on purpose: the requirement, the authoritative paths, the diff, and
   139	// the exact output contract. No conversation history, and — for Codex — no
   140	// account of what the builder believes. A reviewer handed the author's
   141	// justification reviews the justification.
   142	const RECORD_CONTRACT = `
   143	Return ONLY a JSON object, no prose around it, with exactly these fields:
   144	{
   145	  "disposition": "APPROVE" | "APPROVE_WITH_NOTES" | "REJECT",
   146	  "findings": [
   147	    {
   148	      "severity": "CRITICAL"|"HIGH"|"MEDIUM"|"LOW"|"INFORMATIONAL",
   149	      "file": "<repo-relative path>",
   150	      "location": "<line/range/symbol>",
   151	      "problem": "<what is wrong>",
   152	      "evidence": "<the code, output, or spec line that shows it — REQUIRED, non-empty>",
   153	      "impact": "<what breaks — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   154	      "requiredCorrection": "<what must change — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   155	      "verificationMethod": "<what proves the fix — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   156	      "status": "OPEN"
   157	    }
   158	  ],
   159	  "unverified": ["<anything you could not check>"]
   160	}
   161	Rules: a finding with no evidence is an opinion — omit it. Do not manufacture
   162	findings to justify the review; zero findings is a valid, useful result.`;
   163	
   164	// CONFIRMED FINDING #6: prompts carried only the objective, paths and diff.
   165	// The packet requires reviewers to receive authoritative paths, test evidence
   166	// and explicit unverified items — without them a reviewer cannot tell an
   167	// intentional design from an accident, and cannot know which claims were
   168	// actually checked. Bounded: digests and a tail, never whole files.
   169	function evidenceBlock(ctx) {
   170	  if (!ctx) return '';
   171	  const lines = [];
   172	  if (ctx.specs && ctx.specs.length) {
   173	    lines.push('', 'PINNED SPECIFICATIONS (authoritative intent, by content digest):');
   174	    for (const sp of ctx.specs) lines.push(`  ${sp.path}  sha256:${sp.sha.slice(0, 16)}…`);
   175	  }
   176	  if (ctx.checks && ctx.checks.length) {
   177	    lines.push('', 'DETERMINISTIC EVIDENCE (already executed against this subject):');
   178	    for (const c of ctx.checks) lines.push(`  exit ${c.exit}  ${c.cmd}`);
   179	    lines.push('', 'Treat a passing check as evidence that the command ran, NOT that the');
   180	    lines.push('requirement is met. If a test passes but a user would still see wrong');
   181	    lines.push('behaviour, that is exactly the finding worth reporting.');
   182	  }
   183	  if (ctx.unverified && ctx.unverified.length) {
   184	    lines.push('', 'EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED (nobody checked these — do not assume either way):');
   185	    for (const u of ctx.unverified) lines.push(`  - ${u}`);
   186	  }
   187	  return lines.join('\n');
   188	}
   189	
   190	function codexPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   191	  return `You are an independent senior technical lead reviewing a change.
   192	
   193	Do not assume the implementation is correct. Independently determine what
   194	correct behaviour should be from the stated objective, then inspect what was
   195	actually written.
   196	
   197	OBJECTIVE (from the approved task packet):
   198	${objective}
   199	
   200	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   201	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   202	
   203	Look for: architectural drift, incomplete functionality, regression, duplicated
   204	logic, state and concurrency errors, API/contract violations, auth mistakes,
   205	security problems, missing validation, edge cases, mock or hardcoded data,
   206	misleading tests, deleted functionality.
   207	
   208	${evidenceBlock(ctx)}
   209	
   210	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   211	${diff}
   212	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   213	}
   214	
   215	function grokPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   216	  return `You are the final adversarial reviewer. Assume this change contains a
   217	serious flaw that previous engineers missed, and try to prove it incorrect.
   218	
   219	Attack: requirement interpretation, edge cases, regression paths, authorization,
   220	security, data integrity, state, concurrency, error paths, dependency
   221	assumptions, and the tests themselves. Specifically hunt for cases where the
   222	tests PASS but a user still experiences wrong behaviour.
   223	
   224	Do not invent theoretical criticism without evidence.
   225	
   226	OBJECTIVE:
   227	${objective}
   228	
   229	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   230	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   231	
   232	${evidenceBlock(ctx)}
   233	
   234	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   235	${diff}
   236	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   237	}
   238	
   239	// ── raw output -> record ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   240	// Extract the first balanced JSON object. Models wrap JSON in prose and fences
   241	// no matter how firmly the contract says not to; that is a formatting quirk,
   242	// not a reason to discard a real review.
   243	function extractJson(text) {
   244	  if (typeof text !== 'string') return null;
   245	  const start = text.indexOf('{');
   246	  if (start === -1) return null;
   247	  let depth = 0, inStr = false, esc = false;
   248	  for (let i = start; i < text.length; i++) {
   249	    const c = text[i];
   250	    if (inStr) {
   251	      if (esc) esc = false;
   252	      else if (c === '\\') esc = true;
   253	      else if (c === '"') inStr = false;
   254	      continue;
   255	    }
   256	    if (c === '"') inStr = true;
   257	    else if (c === '{') depth++;
   258	    else if (c === '}') {
   259	      depth--;
   260	      if (depth === 0) {
   260	      if (depth === 0) {
   261	        try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(start, i + 1)); } catch { return null; }
   262	      }
   263	    }
   264	  }
   265	  return null;
   266	}
   267	
   268	// A parsed object is only a review if it actually carries a verdict. Some CLIs
   269	// wrap their run in a JSON envelope ({text, stopReason, usage…}); extracting
   270	// that envelope must not be mistaken for extracting a review.
   271	function isUsableReview(o) {
   272	  return !!(o && typeof o === 'object' && typeof o.disposition === 'string');
   273	}
   274	
   275	// Build a record. `parsed` null/unusable => UNAVAILABLE, never APPROVE.
   276	function buildRecord({ reviewer, reviewerModel, packetId, subject, parsed, unavailableReason, ts }) {
   277	  const base = {
   278	    reviewId: `REV-${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-${reviewer}`,
   279	    ts,
   280	    reviewer,
   281	    reviewerModel,
   282	    packetId,
   283	    reviewOf: {
   284	      diffSha256: subject.subjectSha256,
   285	      changedPaths: subject.subjectPaths.slice(),
   286	    },
   287	  };
   288	  if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || !parsed.disposition) {
   289	    return {
   290	      ...base,
   291	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   292	      unavailableReason: unavailableReason || 'the reviewer produced no parseable review record',
   293	      findings: [],
   294	    };
   295	  }
   296	  const allowed = ['APPROVE', 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES', 'REJECT'];
   297	  if (!allowed.includes(parsed.disposition)) {
   298	    return {
   299	      ...base,
   300	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   301	      unavailableReason: `the reviewer returned an unrecognised disposition ${JSON.stringify(parsed.disposition)}`,
   302	      findings: [],
   303	    };
   304	  }
   305	  const findings = Array.isArray(parsed.findings) ? parsed.findings : [];
   306	  return {
   307	    ...base,
   308	    disposition: parsed.disposition,
   309	    findings: findings
   310	      // A finding with empty evidence is dropped rather than passed through to
   311	      // fail schema validation and take the whole record down with it. Dropping
   312	      // an unevidenced finding is safe; dropping an evidenced one never happens.
   313	      .filter((f) => f && typeof f.evidence === 'string' && f.evidence.trim() !== '')
   314	      .map((f) => ({
   315	        severity: f.severity || 'INFORMATIONAL',
   316	        file: f.file || '(unspecified)',
   317	        location: f.location || '',
   318	        problem: f.problem || '',
   319	        evidence: f.evidence,
   320	        impact: f.impact || '',
   321	        requiredCorrection: f.requiredCorrection || '',
   322	        verificationMethod: f.verificationMethod || '',
   323	        status: 'OPEN',
   324	      })),
   325	    unverified: Array.isArray(parsed.unverified) ? parsed.unverified.map(String) : [],
   326	  };
   327	}
   328	
   329	function validateRecord(recordPath) {
   330	  const r = spawnSync('node', [ENGOS, '--validate-review', recordPath, '--json'],
   331	    { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   332	  let parsed = [];
   333	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* fall through */ }
   334	  const first = parsed[0] || { ok: false, errors: ['validator produced no result'] };
   335	  return { ok: !!first.ok, errors: first.errors || [] };
   336	}
   337	
   338	// ── run ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   339	// CONFIRMED FINDING #4: adapters selected hardcoded binaries directly and
   340	// ignored the Tool Capability Canon, so disabling a reviewer in the canon had
   341	// no effect on what actually ran, and dashboard/router state could disagree
   342	// with execution. The canon is now consulted before any tool is launched.
   343	// CONFIRMED FINDING #5: canonGate only rejected enabled=false / DISABLED, so a
   344	// tool whose availability was UNVERIFIED or BLOCKED still ran, and runTool()
   345	// spawned Grok directly without ever consulting routeRole() — meaning the
   346	// metered authorization, data-class veto and role separation in
   347	// MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json governed nothing at execution time. There is now
   348	// ONE authorization path and every launch goes through it.
   349	function authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts = {}) {
   350	  const canon = canonGate(reviewer);
   351	  if (!canon.ok) return canon;
   352	
   353	  // Availability must be positively AVAILABLE with evidence. UNVERIFIED is not
   354	  // a soft yes — it is the absence of a check.
   355	  const avail = canon.tool.availability;
   356	  if (avail !== 'AVAILABLE') {
   357	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is ${avail} in the Tool Capability Canon; only AVAILABLE (with evidence) may be launched` };
   358	  }
   359	  if (!canon.tool.availabilityEvidence || !canon.tool.availabilityEvidence.observedAt) {
   360	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is marked AVAILABLE but carries no dated availability evidence` };
   361	  }
   362	
   363	  // Role routing: data-class veto, metered authorization, role separation.
   364	  const roleId = reviewer === 'grok' ? 'adversarial-review'
   365	    : reviewer === 'codex' ? 'implementation-review'
   366	    : 'repository-guardian';
   367	  let route;
   368	  try {
   369	    route = require('./tool-router.cjs').routeRole(roleId, {
   370	      dataClass: opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL',
   371	      allowMetered: opts.allowMetered,
   372	      approvedBy: opts.approvedBy,
   373	      capUsd: opts.capUsd,
   374	    });
   375	  } catch (e) {
   376	    return { ok: false, reason: `model routing policy unusable: ${e.message}` };
   377	  }
   378	  if (!route.ok) return { ok: false, reason: `${route.code}: ${route.reason}` };
   379	  return { ok: true, tool: canon.tool, route };
   380	}
   381	
   382	function canonGate(reviewer) {
   383	  const fsx = require('fs');
   384	  const canonPath = require('path').join(HERE, 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json');
   385	  if (!fsx.existsSync(canonPath)) {
   386	    return { ok: false, reason: 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json is missing — refusing to run a reviewer the canon cannot authorize' };
   387	  }
   388	  let canon;
   389	  try { canon = JSON.parse(fsx.readFileSync(canonPath, 'utf8')); }
   390	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}` }; }
   391	  const wanted = { codex: 'codex-local', grok: 'grok-cli' }[reviewer];
   392	  const tool = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === wanted);
   393	  if (!tool) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is not declared in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   394	  if (tool.enabled === false) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is DISABLED in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   395	  if (tool.availability === 'DISABLED') return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is DISABLED in the canon` };
   396	  return { ok: true, tool };
   397	}
   398	
   399	// Build the exact argument vector a reviewer will be launched with. Exported
   400	// so a test can assert that every bound the policy DECLARES is actually
   401	// PRESENT on the command line — the gap that let maxTurns:1 run 13 turns.
   402	function buildToolArgv(reviewer, prompt, bounds) {
   403	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   404	    return ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   405	  }
   406	  const argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   407	  if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   408	  if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   409	  return argv;
   410	}
   411	
   412	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, opts = {}) {
   413	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   414	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   415	  const gateResult = authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts);
   416	  if (!gateResult.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gateResult.reason, raw: '' };
   417	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   418	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   419	  }
   420	  let argv;
   421	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   422	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   423	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   424	  } else {
   425	    // Bounds come from MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json, not from this file, so the
   426	    // Product Owner can tighten an adversarial reviewer without editing code.
   427	    // Single-turn print mode, no approval flags, no web, no sub-agents: the
   428	    // adversary gets exactly one pass at the diff and no way to spend the
   429	    // budget on a conversation with itself.
   430	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   431	    let bounds = null;
   432	    try {
   433	      bounds = (require('./tool-router.cjs').loadPolicy().roles['adversarial-review'] || {}).bounds || null;
   434	    } catch { /* absent policy is handled by the router's own refusal */ }
   435	    // CONFIRMED DEFECT (2026-08-24, caught by reading a real run's telemetry):
   436	    // the policy declared maxTurns:1 and this adapter never passed it. Grok ran
   437	    // 13 turns and returned no record. A bound that is declared in a policy but
   438	    // not passed to the tool is theatre — it reads as governance in the JSON and
   439	    // enforces nothing at runtime. Every bound below is now wired to a real flag
   440	    // or refuses, and buildToolArgv() is asserted against the policy by test.
   441	    if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) {
   442	      argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   443	    }
   444	    if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   445	    // Sub-agents are disabled by OMITTING --agents entirely. Passing an empty
   446	    // value was a bug: the CLI expects a map and rejected the array outright,
   447	    // which failed the launch at argument parsing. No definitions supplied is
   448	    // the correct way to express "no sub-agents".
   449	    if (bounds && bounds.subagents === true) {
   450	      throw new Error('policy enables sub-agents for adversarial-review; this adapter deliberately does not implement that path');
   451	    }
   452	  }
   453	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   454	    encoding: 'utf8',
   455	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   456	    maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
   457	    cwd: ROOT,
   458	  });
   459	  const raw = (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr ? `\n--- stderr ---\n${r.stderr}` : '');
   460	  if (r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') {
   620	
   621	  --doctor [--json]
   622	        Which reviewers are actually installed, at which absolute path.
   623	
   624	  --run --reviewer codex|grok|copilot --packet <p>
   625	        [--subject-sha <sha>] [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>]
   626	        [--timeout <seconds>] [--dry-run]
   627	        Run a reviewer read-only against the bound subject diff, preserve raw
   628	        output under review-raw/, and write a validated record under reviews/.
   629	
   630	No path in this file emits APPROVE unless a reviewer actually said so.
   631	`);
   632	  return EXIT_USAGE;
   633	}
   634	
   635	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   636	function parseArgs(argv) {
   637	  const a = {};
   638	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   639	    const t = argv[i];
   640	    if (t === '--reviewer') a.reviewer = argv[++i];
   641	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   642	    else if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   643	    else if (t === '--base') a.base = argv[++i];
   644	    else if (t === '--head') a.head = argv[++i];
   645	    else if (t === '--timeout') a.timeout = argv[++i];
   646	    else if (t === '--dry-run') a.dryRun = true;
   647	    else if (t === '--data-class') a.dataClass = argv[++i];
   648	    else if (t === '--allow-metered') a.allowMetered = true;
   649	    else if (t === '--approved-by') a.approvedBy = argv[++i];
   650	    else if (t === '--cap-usd') a.capUsd = argv[++i];
   651	    else if (t === '--only-path') (a.onlyPaths = a.onlyPaths || []).push(argv[++i]);
   652	    else if (t === '--group-id') a.groupId = argv[++i];
   653	    else if (t === '--group-digest') a.groupDigest = argv[++i];
   654	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   655	    else if (t === '--doctor') a.doctor = true;
   656	    else if (t === '--run') a.run = true;
   657	  }
   658	  return a;
   659	}
   660	
   661	if (require.main === module) {
   662	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   663	  let code;
   664	  try {
   665	    if (args.doctor) code = cmdDoctor(args);
   666	    else if (args.run) code = cmdRun(args);
   667	    else code = usage();
   668	  } catch (e) {
   669	    process.stderr.write(`\n[review-adapters] ${e.message}\n`);
   670	    code = EXIT_BLOCK;
   671	  }
   672	  process.exit(code);
   673	}
   674

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * install-engineering-os.cjs — install the Engineering OS into another repo,
     4	 * or refuse and say exactly what is missing.
     5	 *
     6	 * WHY THIS EXISTS
     7	 * The first version of the install instructions were a `cp` list in a Markdown
     8	 * file. That was dishonest by omission: engineering-os.cjs shells out to
     9	 * packet-tools.cjs, ledger-writer.cjs and protected-paths.json, so copying the
    10	 * five "Engineering OS" files into a repo without those produces a CLI that
    11	 * looks installed, runs, and fails at the first gate — or worse, appears to
    12	 * work because the failure is on a path nobody exercised yet.
    13	 *
    14	 * So this installs the COMPLETE required package, or it refuses. There is no
    15	 * partial install, because a partial install of a gate is indistinguishable
    16	 * from a working one right up until it matters.
    17	 *
    18	 *   node builder-control/install-engineering-os.cjs --doctor [--target <dir>] [--json]
    19	 *   node builder-control/install-engineering-os.cjs --install --target <dir> [--force]
    20	 *
    21	 * Exit: 0 ok · 2 usage · 3 refused (prerequisites absent, or target unsafe)
    22	 */
    23	'use strict';
    24	
    25	const fs = require('fs');
    26	const path = require('path');
    27	
    28	const HERE = __dirname;
    29	const SOURCE_ROOT = path.resolve(HERE, '..');
    30	
    31	const EXIT_PASS = 0;
    32	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    33	const EXIT_REFUSED = 3;
    34	
    35	// The canonical Builder Control pieces this CLI actually calls at runtime.
    36	// Every entry here is a real dependency, verified by reading the source: each
    37	// is either require()d, spawned, or read by engineering-os.cjs or the code it
    38	// invokes. Nothing is listed aspirationally.
    39	const PREREQS = [
    40	  { p: 'builder-control/packet-tools.cjs', why: 'engineering-os spawns it to validate a task packet' },
    41	  { p: 'builder-control/ledger-writer.cjs', why: 'engineering-os spawns it to append ledger entries' },
    42	  { p: 'builder-control/protected-paths.json', why: 'the risk classifier reads it; absent = hard block by design' },
    43	  { p: 'builder-control/agent-registry.json', why: 'packet-tools validates agentId against it' },
    44	  { p: 'builder-control/schemas/task-packet.schema.json', why: 'packet validation contract' },
    45	  { p: 'builder-control/schemas/ledger-entry.schema.json', why: 'ledger append contract' },
    46	  { p: 'builder-control/gate.cjs', why: 'the HARD-BLOCK gate the pre-flight composes' },
    47	  { p: 'builder-control/preflight.cjs', why: 'the one mandatory dispatch pre-flight command' },
    48	  { p: 'builder-control/boundary-checks.cjs', why: 'gate.cjs requires it' },
    49	];
    50

   101	const exists = (root, rel) => fs.existsSync(path.join(root, rel));
   102	
   103	function auditSource() {
   104	  return {
   105	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, d.p) })),
   106	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, p) })),
   107	  };
   108	}
   109	
   110	function auditTarget(target) {
   111	  return {
   112	    isDir: fs.existsSync(target) && fs.statSync(target).isDirectory(),
   113	    isGit: fs.existsSync(path.join(target, '.git')),
   114	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(target, d.p) })),
   115	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(target, p) })),
   116	  };
   117	}
   118	
   119	function cmdDoctor(args) {
   120	  const src = auditSource();
   121	  const tgt = args.target ? auditTarget(args.target) : null;
   122	  const missingSrcPrereq = src.prereqs.filter((d) => !d.present);
   123	  const missingSrcOs = src.osFiles.filter((d) => !d.present);
   124	
   125	  if (args.json) {
   126	    console.log(JSON.stringify({ source: src, target: tgt, ok: missingSrcPrereq.length === 0 }, null, 2));
   127	    return missingSrcPrereq.length ? EXIT_REFUSED : EXIT_PASS;
   128	  }
   129	
   130	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — INSTALL DOCTOR');
   131	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   132	  console.log(`source: ${SOURCE_ROOT}`);
   133	  console.log('');
   134	  console.log('Builder Control prerequisites (this CLI calls these at runtime):');
   135	  for (const d of src.prereqs) {
   136	    console.log(`  ${d.present ? 'ok     ' : 'MISSING'} ${d.p}`);
   137	    if (!d.present) console.log(`          ${d.why}`);
   138	  }
   139	  console.log('');
   140	  console.log('Engineering OS layer:');
   141	  for (const d of src.osFiles) console.log(`  ${d.present ? 'ok     ' : 'MISSING'} ${d.p}`);
   142	
   143	  if (tgt) {
   144	    console.log('');
   145	    console.log(`target: ${args.target}`);
   146	    console.log(`  directory : ${tgt.isDir ? 'yes' : 'NO — does not exist'}`);
   147	    console.log(`  git repo  : ${tgt.isGit ? 'yes' : 'no (not fatal, but the gate binds to git refs)'}`);
   148	    const tMissing = tgt.prereqs.filter((d) => !d.present);
   149	    console.log(`  prereqs   : ${tgt.prereqs.length - tMissing.length}/${tgt.prereqs.length} present`);
   150	    const tOs = tgt.osFiles.filter((d) => d.present);
   151	    console.log(`  OS layer  : ${tOs.length}/${tgt.osFiles.length} present`);
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  console.log('');
   155	  if (missingSrcPrereq.length) {
   156	    console.log(`REFUSED: ${missingSrcPrereq.length} prerequisite(s) missing from the SOURCE repo.`);
   157	    console.log('There is nothing complete here to install.');
   158	    return EXIT_REFUSED;
   159	  }
   160	  if (missingSrcOs.length) {
   161	    console.log(`WARNING: ${missingSrcOs.length} Engineering OS file(s) not present in the source.`);
   162	    console.log('An install would copy an incomplete layer. Fix the source first.');
   163	    return EXIT_REFUSED;
   164	  }
   165	  console.log('Source is complete. An install would copy the full required package.');
   166	  return EXIT_PASS;
   167	}
   168	
   169	function cmdInstall(args) {
   170	  if (!args.target) { process.stderr.write('\n[install] --target <dir> is required\n'); return EXIT_USAGE; }
   171	  const target = path.resolve(args.target);
   172	
   173	  if (path.resolve(target) === path.resolve(SOURCE_ROOT)) {
   174	    process.stderr.write('\n[install] REFUSED: target is the source repo.\n');
   175	    return EXIT_REFUSED;
   176	  }
   177	  if (!fs.existsSync(target) || !fs.statSync(target).isDirectory()) {
   178	    process.stderr.write(`\n[install] REFUSED: target is not an existing directory: ${target}\n`);
   179	    return EXIT_REFUSED;
   180	  }
   181	
   182	  // Refuse on an incomplete source rather than produce a half-installed gate.
   183	  const src = auditSource();
   184	  const missing = [...src.prereqs.filter((d) => !d.present), ...src.osFiles.filter((d) => !d.present)];
   185	  if (missing.length) {
   186	    process.stderr.write('\n[install] REFUSED — the source repo is missing required files:\n');
   187	    for (const d of missing) process.stderr.write(`    ${d.p}${d.why ? `  (${d.why})` : ''}\n`);
   188	    process.stderr.write('\nA partial install of a gate is indistinguishable from a working one\nuntil it matters. Nothing was copied.\n');
   189	    return EXIT_REFUSED;
   190	  }
   191	
   192	  const all = [...PREREQS.map((d) => d.p), ...OS_FILES];
   193	  const wrote = [];
   194	  const skipped = [];
   195	  for (const rel of all) {
   196	    const from = path.join(SOURCE_ROOT, rel);
   197	    const to = path.join(target, rel);
   198	    if (fs.existsSync(to) && !args.force) { skipped.push(rel); continue; }
   199	    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(to), { recursive: true });
   200	    fs.copyFileSync(from, to);
   201	    try { fs.chmodSync(to, fs.statSync(from).mode); } catch { /* best effort */ }
   202	    wrote.push(rel);
   203	  }
   204	
   205	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — INSTALL');
   206	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   207	  console.log(`target: ${target}`);
   208	  console.log(`copied : ${wrote.length}`);
   209	  for (const w of wrote) console.log(`  + ${w}`);
   210	  if (skipped.length) {
   211	    console.log(`kept   : ${skipped.length} existing file(s) left untouched (use --force to overwrite)`);
   212	    for (const s of skipped) console.log(`  = ${s}`);
   213	  }
   214	  console.log('');
   215	  console.log('NEXT — none of this is done for you, and none of it is claimed:');
   216	  console.log('  1. Verify:  node builder-control/test/engineering-os.test.cjs');
   217	  console.log('               node builder-control/test/tool-router.test.cjs');
   218	  console.log('  2. Copy the "Engineering OS —" steps into the target CI workflow.');
   219	  console.log('  3. Review protected-paths.json: it was copied from the source repo');
   220	  console.log('     and lists SOURCE paths. It must be rewritten for the target, or');
   221	  console.log('     the target will protect files it does not have and miss its own.');
   222	  console.log('  4. Reviewer binaries are machine-local and were NOT installed.');
   223	  console.log('  5. The attestation key is machine-local and was NOT copied.');
   224	  console.log('     The first signed review generates one at builder-control/.attestation-key');
   225	  console.log('     Add that path to the target .gitignore before signing anything.');
   226	  console.log('     Run: node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor');
   227	  return EXIT_PASS;
   228	}
   229	
   230	function parseArgs(argv) {
   231	  const a = {};
   232	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   233	    const t = argv[i];
   234	    if (t === '--target') a.target = argv[++i];
   235	    else if (t === '--install') a.install = true;
   236	    else if (t === '--doctor') a.doctor = true;
   237	    else if (t === '--force') a.force = true;
   238	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   239	  }
   240	  return a;
   241	}
   242	
   243	if (require.main === module) {
   244	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   245	  let code;
   246	  if (args.doctor) code = cmdDoctor(args);
   247	  else if (args.install) code = cmdInstall(args);
   248	  else {
   249	    process.stderr.write(`
   250	install-engineering-os.cjs
   251	
   252	  --doctor [--target <dir>] [--json]
   253	        What is present, what is missing, whether an install could succeed.
   254	
   255	  --install --target <dir> [--force]
   256	        Copy the COMPLETE required package (Builder Control prerequisites +
   257	        Engineering OS layer) into the target repo, or refuse and say why.
   258	
   259	Exit: 0 ok · 2 usage · 3 refused
   260	`);
   261	    code = EXIT_USAGE;
   262	  }
   263	  process.exit(code);
   264	}
   265	
   266	module.exports = { PREREQS, OS_FILES, auditSource, auditTarget, SOURCE_ROOT };
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	'use strict';
     3	
     4	const assert = require('assert');
     5	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
     6	const fs = require('fs');
     7	const os = require('os');
     8	const path = require('path');
     9	const { PREREQS, OS_FILES, auditSource, SOURCE_ROOT } = require('../install-engineering-os.cjs');
    10	
    11	const CLI = path.join(SOURCE_ROOT, 'builder-control', 'install-engineering-os.cjs');
    12	const TMP = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'engos-install-test-'));
    13	process.on('exit', () => { try { fs.rmSync(TMP, { recursive: true, force: true }); } catch {} });
    14	
    15	let passed = 0;
    16	function test(name, fn) {
    17	  try { fn(); passed++; console.log(`ok   ${name}`); }
    18	  catch (e) { console.error(`FAIL ${name}: ${e.message}`); process.exitCode = 1; }
    19	}
    20	function run(args) {
    21	  return spawnSync('node', [CLI, ...args], { cwd: SOURCE_ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
    22	}
    23	
    24	console.log('Engineering OS — installer fixtures');
    25	
    26	test('source package is complete', () => {
    27	  const audit = auditSource();
    28	  assert.deepStrictEqual(audit.prereqs.filter((x) => !x.present), []);
    29	  assert.deepStrictEqual(audit.osFiles.filter((x) => !x.present), []);
    30	});
    31	
    32	test('doctor passes on the source package', () => {
    33	  const r = run(['--doctor']);
    34	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 0, `${r.stdout}\n${r.stderr}`);
    35	  assert.ok(r.stdout.includes('Source is complete'));
    36	});
    37	
    38	test('installer copies every required file to an empty target', () => {
    39	  const target = path.join(TMP, 'target');
    40	  fs.mkdirSync(target);
    41	  const r = run(['--install', '--target', target]);
    42	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 0, `${r.stdout}\n${r.stderr}`);
    43	  for (const item of [...PREREQS.map((x) => x.p), ...OS_FILES]) {
    44	    assert.ok(fs.existsSync(path.join(target, item)), `missing ${item}`);
    45	  }
    46	});
    47	
    48	test('installer refuses to install onto its source repo', () => {
    49	  const r = run(['--install', '--target', SOURCE_ROOT]);
    50	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 3);
    51	  assert.ok(r.stderr.includes('target is the source repo'));
    52	});
    53	
    54	// RED PROOF — the installer must REFUSE when prerequisites are absent.
    55	// A partial install of a gate is indistinguishable from a working one right up
    56	// until it matters, so "copy what exists and hope" is the one behaviour that
    57	// must not be possible. This builds a genuinely incomplete source repo (the
    58	// installer resolves SOURCE_ROOT from its own __dirname) and proves it refuses.
    59	test('installer REFUSES from a source missing its Builder Control prerequisites', () => {
    60	  const fakeSrc = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(TMP, 'fake-src-'));
    61	  const target = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(TMP, 'fake-target-'));
    62	  fs.mkdirSync(path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control'), { recursive: true });
    63	  // Only the installer itself is present. Every prerequisite is missing.
    64	  fs.copyFileSync(CLI, path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control', 'install-engineering-os.cjs'));
    65	
    66	  const r = spawnSync('node',
    67	    [path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control', 'install-engineering-os.cjs'), '--install', '--target', target],
    68	    { encoding: 'utf8' });
    69	
    70	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 3, `expected refusal exit 3, got ${r.status}`);
    71	  assert.ok(/REFUSED/.test(r.stderr), 'refusal must say REFUSED');
    72	  assert.ok(/packet-tools\.cjs/.test(r.stderr), 'refusal must name the missing prerequisite');
    73	  assert.ok(/Nothing was copied/.test(r.stderr), 'refusal must state that nothing was copied');
    74	
    75	  // And it must have actually copied nothing.
    76	  const leaked = fs.existsSync(path.join(target, 'builder-control'));
    77	  assert.ok(!leaked, 'a refused install must leave the target untouched');
    78	});
    79	
    80	test('installer doctor REFUSES (exit 3) on an incomplete source', () => {
    81	  const fakeSrc = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(TMP, 'fake-doc-'));
    82	  fs.mkdirSync(path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control'), { recursive: true });
    83	  fs.copyFileSync(CLI, path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control', 'install-engineering-os.cjs'));
    84	  const r = spawnSync('node',
    85	    [path.join(fakeSrc, 'builder-control', 'install-engineering-os.cjs'), '--doctor'],
    86	    { encoding: 'utf8' });
    87	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 3, `expected exit 3, got ${r.status}`);
    88	  assert.ok(/MISSING/.test(r.stdout), 'doctor must list what is missing');
    89	});
    90	
    91	test('every declared prerequisite is a real runtime dependency that exists here', () => {
    92	  for (const d of PREREQS) {
    93	    assert.ok(fs.existsSync(path.join(SOURCE_ROOT, d.p)), `declared prerequisite absent: ${d.p}`);
    94	    assert.ok(d.why && d.why.length > 0, `prerequisite ${d.p} has no stated reason`);
    95	  }
    96	  assert.ok(OS_FILES.length > 0);
    97	});
    98	
    99	const failedCount = process.exitCode ? 'at least 1' : '0';
   100	console.log(`${passed} passed, ${failedCount} failed.`);
     1	name: Builder Control — enforcement self-test
     2	
     3	# This workflow tests THE ENFORCEMENT, not the app.
     4	#
     5	# Layers 1 and 4 are checks that exist to catch other people's mistakes, which
     6	# makes them the easiest things in the repo to break without noticing: nothing
     7	# fails when a validator silently stops validating. So CI runs their own test
     8	# suites — the fixtures that prove each rule still fires, and the throwaway-repo
     9	# experiments that prove a --no-verify commit is still visible.
    10	#
    11	# It also runs server-side, which is the point. A local hook can be skipped with
    12	# one flag; this cannot. On 2026-07-28 a --no-verify commit followed by a hard
    13	# reset destroyed 178 staged files while every local check reported green.
    14	
    15	on:
    16	  push:
    17	    paths:
    18	      - 'builder-control/**'
    19	      - 'scripts/audit-protocol-content-contract.mjs'
    20	      - 'scripts/test/**'
    21	      - '.github/workflows/builder-control.yml'
    22	      # The Engineering OS instruction surfaces. Editing what the reviewers are
    23	      # told is a change to the enforcement, so it re-runs the enforcement.
    24	      - '.github/copilot-instructions.md'
    25	      - '.github/agents/**'
    26	      - '.github/pull_request_template.md'
    27	      - 'AGENTS.md'
    28	      - 'CLAUDE.md'
    29	  # No path filter on pull_request — see vitalis-app.yml for the reasoning.
    30	  # `main` requires this check, and a required check that never reports is a
    31	  # deadlock, not a safeguard.
    32	  pull_request:
    33	  workflow_dispatch:
    34	
    35	jobs:
    36	  enforcement:
    37	    name: doctrine validators + bypass telemetry
    38	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    39	    steps:
    40	      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    41	        with:
    42	          # The doctrine step diffs against the push/PR base. checkout is shallow
    43	          # by default, so that base object is not in the clone and `git diff`
    44	          # exits 128 with "bad object" — which reads as a doctrine failure when
    45	          # it is really a missing commit.
    46	          fetch-depth: 0
    47	
    48	      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
    49	        with:
    50	          node-version: '22'
    51	
    52	      - name: Layer 4 — is post-commit still a valid bypass observer?
    53	        # Re-measured against CI's git version rather than trusted from the Mac
    54	        # it was first measured on. If a future git ever skips post-commit under
    55	        # --no-verify, Layer 4's placement becomes invalid and this must fail.
    56	        run: bash builder-control/test/bypass-experiment.sh
    57	
    58	      - name: Layer 4 — bypass telemetry fails closed
    59	        run: bash builder-control/test/layer4-telemetry.sh
    60	
    61	      - name: Layer 1 — doctrine validators fire on their fixtures
    62	        run: bash scripts/test/doctrine-validators.sh
    63	
    64	      - name: Layer 1 — doctrine rules apply to what THIS change touches
    65	        # Changed files, not the whole corpus. There are ~105 pre-existing
    66	        # violations in client documents written before these rules were
    67	        # executable; running --all here would make CI red on arrival, and a
    68	        # permanently-red gate is one nobody reads. This blocks NEW drift and
    69	        # leaves the backlog to a deliberate cleanup.
    70	        #
    71	        # It still fails closed: a missing or unparseable registry exits 2, so a
    72	        # deleted rulebook cannot read as "no violations".
    73	        run: |
    74	          set -euo pipefail
    75	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
    76	          # Fall back to HEAD's own files whenever the base is absent, empty, or
    77	          # not present in this clone (force-push, first push of a branch, or a
    78	          # shallow fetch). Checking THIS commit's files is the honest degraded
    79	          # mode; skipping the check would not be.
    80	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
    81	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
    82	            echo "Base '$BASE' unavailable — checking HEAD's own files instead."
    83	            FILES=$(git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD)
    84	          else
    85	            FILES=$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" HEAD)
    86	          fi
    87	          if [ -z "$FILES" ]; then
    88	            echo "No changed files."; exit 0
    89	          fi
    90	          echo "$FILES"
    91	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
    92	          node builder-control/doctrine-check.cjs $FILES
    93	
    94	      - name: Layer 2 — one owner per job
    95	        run: node builder-control/single-authority-check.cjs
    96	
    97	      # ── Engineering OS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    98	      # The multi-model build loop (Claude builds, Codex reviews, Copilot
    99	      # guards, Grok attacks) is only as good as the part of it that cannot be
   100	      # talked out of a verdict. These steps are that part. They run on every
   101	      # push and PR so the workflow activates itself — nobody has to remember
   102	      # to invoke it, and nobody has to paste a protocol into a chat window.
   103	      #
   104	      # Every step here fails the job when it fails. There is no report-only
   105	      # mode and no environment-variable opt-in: a gate that can be switched
   106	      # off from repository settings is a suggestion, and the first time it
   107	      # matters is exactly when someone will be tempted to switch it off.
   108	
   109	      - name: Engineering OS — rule fixtures still fire
   110	        # A classifier that silently stopped classifying would pass every build
   111	        # and look identical to a healthy one. These fixtures feed it changes
   112	        # that MUST be refused, and fail if any of them is let through.
   113	        run: node builder-control/test/engineering-os.test.cjs
   114	
   115	      - name: Engineering OS — router, adapter, installer, state and slice fixtures
   116	        # The router decides which tool is allowed to do a job, the adapters
   117	        # decide what counts as a review, and the installer decides whether a
   118	        # copy of this system is complete. All three fail silently when broken —
   119	        # a router with an empty canon routes nothing and reports no error — so
   120	        # each one carries fixtures that must be refused.
   121	        run: |
   122	          set -euo pipefail
   123	          node builder-control/test/tool-router.test.cjs
   124	          node builder-control/test/review-adapters.test.cjs
   125	          node builder-control/test/install-engineering-os.test.cjs
   126	          node builder-control/test/model-routing.test.cjs
   127	          node builder-control/test/knowledge-mirror.test.cjs
   128	          node builder-control/test/hosting.test.cjs
   129	          node builder-control/test/review-chunker.test.cjs
   130	          node builder-control/test/aegis-state.test.cjs
   131	          # CONFIRMED FINDING (#3): dashboard/state.js is generated and
   132	          # gitignored, so a clean runner has no copy and the slice test failed
   133	          # on arrival. Generate it here — which also proves the generator
   134	          # itself still runs, rather than only testing a file someone left
   135	          # lying around locally.
   136	          node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js
   137	          node builder-control/test/dashboard-slice.test.cjs
   138	
   139	      - name: Engineering OS — compute the subject once
   140	        # ONE computation of subject paths and subject hash, reused by every
   141	        # step below. Computing it twice invites two answers, and the whole
   142	        # value of a bound review is that there is exactly one thing it is
   143	        # bound to.
   144	        id: subject
   145	        run: |
   146	          set -euo pipefail
   147	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
   148	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
   149	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
   150	            # CONFIRMED FINDING #2: this used `--subject` with no refs, which
   151	            # runs `git diff HEAD` — EMPTY on a clean Actions checkout. The
   152	            # empty subject was then classified LIGHT and skipped the binding
   153	            # gate entirely, so the fallback silently disabled enforcement on
   154	            # exactly the runs that needed it. HEAD's own file list is now
   155	            # enumerated explicitly, and an empty result fails closed below.
   156	            echo "Base '$BASE' unavailable — enumerating HEAD's own files."
   157	            USED_BASE=""
   158	            HEADFILES=$(git diff-tree --root --no-commit-id -r --name-only HEAD | sed '/^$/d')
   159	            if [ -z "$HEADFILES" ]; then
   160	              echo "BLOCKED: no trustworthy file range could be established for HEAD."
   161	              echo "An unknown subject is never treated as a small one."
   162	              exit 1
   163	            fi
   164	            CHANGEDARGS=()
   165	            while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && CHANGEDARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done <<< "$HEADFILES"
   166	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject "${CHANGEDARGS[@]}" --json)
   167	          else
   168	            USED_BASE="$BASE"
   169	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject --base "$BASE" --head HEAD --json)
   170	          fi
   171	          echo "$SUBJ"
   172	          SHA=$(echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).subjectSha256))')
   173	          echo "sha=$SHA" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   174	          echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{const j=JSON.parse(s);console.log(j.subjectPaths.join("\n"))})' > /tmp/subject-paths.txt
   175	          echo "count=$(wc -l < /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr -d " ")" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   176	          # Record the EXACT base used, so the gate step certifies the same
   177	          # range rather than recomputing a different one.
   178	          echo "base=$USED_BASE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   179	
   180	      - name: Engineering OS — bind to exactly one task packet
   181	        # A change that carries two modified packets has two competing
   182	        # authorizations and no deterministic way to choose between them.
   183	        # Rather than pick one, this blocks and says so.
   184	        id: packet
   185	        run: |
   186	          set -euo pipefail
   187	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
   188	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
   189	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
   190	            CHANGED=$(git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD)
   191	          else
   192	            CHANGED=$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" HEAD)
   193	          fi
   194	          PACKETS=$(echo "$CHANGED" | grep -E '^builder-control/packets/.*\.json$' || true)
   195	          COUNT=$(if [ -z "$PACKETS" ]; then echo 0; else echo "$PACKETS" | wc -l | tr -d ' '; fi)
   196	          echo "changed packets: $COUNT"
   197	          echo "$PACKETS"
   198	          if [ "$COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
   199	            echo ''
   200	            echo 'BLOCKED: this change modifies more than one task packet.'
   201	            echo 'Exactly one packet authorizes a change. Two packets means two'
   202	            echo 'answers to "what was this allowed to touch", and there is no'
   203	            echo 'honest way to pick. Split the change.'
   204	            exit 1
   205	          fi
   206	          if [ "$COUNT" -eq 1 ]; then
   207	            echo "active=$PACKETS" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   208	            echo "── pinning intent for $PACKETS"
   209	            node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --spec-check --packet "$PACKETS"
   210	          else
   211	            echo "active=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   212	            echo "No task packet added or modified by this change."
   213	          fi
   214	
   215	      - name: Engineering OS — classify the subject
   216	        id: classify
   217	        run: |
   218	          set -euo pipefail
   219	          SUBJECT_BASE="${{ steps.subject.outputs.base }}"
   220	          ARGS=()
   221	          while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && ARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done < /tmp/subject-paths.txt
   222	          if [ ${#ARGS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
   223	            # FINDING #2: an empty subject used to be declared LIGHT, which is
   224	            # how a computation failure became a governance bypass. Zero subject
   225	            # paths after a successful subject computation means the change is
   226	            # evidence-only; that is reported, and the lane is left UNKNOWN so
   227	            # the gate step still runs and decides.
   228	            echo "No subject paths — evidence-only change. Lane left UNKNOWN; the gate still runs."
   229	            echo "lane=UNKNOWN" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   230	            exit 0
   231	          fi
   232	          # The classifier now refuses LIGHT without a line count (finding #10),
   233	          # so CI must supply the real one rather than let an unknown size pass.
   234	          if [ -n "$SUBJECT_BASE" ]; then
   235	            LINES=$(git diff --numstat "$SUBJECT_BASE" HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
   236	          else
   237	            LINES=$(git diff --numstat HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
   238	          fi
   239	          echo "subject changed lines: $LINES"
   240	          node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --classify "${ARGS[@]}" --diff-lines "$LINES" | tee /tmp/classify.txt
   241	          LANE=$(awk -F': *' '/^lane /{print $2; exit}' /tmp/classify.txt | awk '{print $1}')
   242	          echo "lane=$LANE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   243	          {
   244	            echo "### Engineering OS — change classification"
   245	            echo ''
   246	            echo '```'
   247	            cat /tmp/classify.txt
   248	            echo '```'
   249	          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
   250	
   251	      - name: Engineering OS — required review evidence (binding)
   252	        # Fail-closed. Missing review evidence blocks the merge. A review bound
   253	        # to a different subject hash does not count. An unavailable reviewer
   254	        # blocks and is reported as UNAVAILABLE rather than upgraded to a pass.
   255	        #
   256	        # --subject-sha is passed explicitly and is mandatory in the CLI: a gate
   257	        # decision with no bound subject certifies nothing at all.
   258	        # Runs for FULL and for UNKNOWN. Only a positively-classified LIGHT skips.
   259	        if: steps.classify.outputs.lane != 'LIGHT'
   260	        run: |

exec

     8	  AEGIS vertical visual slice #1 — Workflow Topology + Process Drift.
     9	
    10	  RULES THIS FILE OBEYS, AND HOW TO BREAK IT ON PURPOSE IF YOU WANT TO CHECK:
    11	
    12	  1. It renders ONLY window.AEGIS_STATE, produced by aegis-state.cjs. There is
    13	     no second data path, no seed data, and no placeholder object. Delete
    14	     state.js and the page renders UNAVAILABLE everywhere rather than a
    15	     confident empty dashboard.

    17	     There is no setInterval anywhere in this file.

    19	     UNAVAILABLE (no source), STALE (source older than threshold, age shown),
    20	     UNVERIFIED (nobody checked).

    22	  5. 2D is authoritative. There is no WebGL here at all, so "works with 3D
    23	     disabled" is not a fallback story, it is the only story.

    56	  main{display:grid;grid-template-columns:minmax(0,1fr) 380px;gap:calc(var(--sp)*4);
    57	    padding:calc(var(--sp)*5);align-items:start}
    58	  .col{display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:calc(var(--sp)*4);min-width:0}
    59	  section{background:var(--bg-1);border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:var(--radius)}
    60	  section > h2{font-size:12px;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;color:var(--text-1);
    61	    padding:calc(var(--sp)*3) calc(var(--sp)*4);border-bottom:1px solid var(--line)}
    62	  .body{padding:calc(var(--sp)*4)}
    63	
    64	  /* ── state chips ────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
    65	  .chip{display:inline-flex;align-items:center;gap:calc(var(--sp)*1.5);
    66	    padding:2px calc(var(--sp)*2);border-radius:999px;font-size:11px;font-weight:600;
    67	    letter-spacing:.04em;border:1px solid currentColor;white-space:nowrap}
    68	  .chip .g{font-size:10px;line-height:1}
    69	  .s-PASS,.s-HEALTHY,.s-AVAILABLE,.s-FRESH{color:var(--pass)}
    70	  .s-ACTIVE{color:var(--active)}
    71	  .s-WARNING,.s-DEGRADED,.s-STALE{color:var(--warn)}
    72	  .s-FAILED,.s-FAIL,.s-MISSING{color:var(--fail)}
    73	  .s-BLOCKED{color:var(--blocked)}
    74	  .s-UNVERIFIED,.s-UNKNOWN,.s-NOT_REQUIRED{color:var(--unknown)}
    75	    /* FINDING #9: readiness states get their own muted treatment — they are
    76	       explicitly NOT passes, and colouring them like one is how a dashboard
    77	       tells its first lie. CONFLICT is loud because it needs a human. */
    78	    .chip.s-READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION,.chip.s-READY_FOR_PR{background:rgba(120,140,190,.14);color:#aebbdb;border-color:rgba(120,140,190,.34)}
    79	    .chip.s-INCOMPLETE,.chip.s-PENDING{background:rgba(150,150,160,.12);color:#a8adba;border-color:rgba(150,150,160,.3)}
    80	    .chip.s-IMPORTED,.chip.s-OK,.chip.s-SYNCED{background:rgba(110,160,140,.13);color:#9fc6b4;border-color:rgba(110,160,140,.3)}
    81	    .chip.s-CONFLICT{background:rgba(214,158,46,.16);color:#e0b055;border-color:rgba(214,158,46,.38)}
    82	    .chip.s-REFUSED{background:rgba(190,90,90,.14);color:#d99;border-color:rgba(190,90,90,.34)}
    83	  .s-UNAVAILABLE,.s-DISCONNECTED,.s-AUTH_EXPIRED{color:var(--stale)}
    84	
    85	  /* ── topology ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
    86	  .stages{list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0;display:flex;flex-direction:column}
    87	  .stage{display:grid;grid-template-columns:28px minmax(0,1fr) auto;gap:calc(var(--sp)*3);
    88	    align-items:center;padding:calc(var(--sp)*3);border:1px solid transparent;
    89	    border-radius:var(--radius);cursor:pointer;background:none;color:inherit;
    90	    font:inherit;text-align:left;width:100%}
    91	  .stage:hover{background:var(--bg-2)}
    92	  .stage:focus-visible{outline:2px solid var(--focus);outline-offset:2px}
    93	  .stage[aria-current="true"]{background:var(--bg-2);border-color:var(--line)}
    94	  .rail{position:relative;display:flex;justify-content:center;height:100%;min-height:34px}
    95	  .rail::before{content:"";position:absolute;top:0;bottom:0;width:2px;background:var(--line)}
    96	  .stage:first-child .rail::before{top:50%}
    97	  .stage:last-child .rail::before{bottom:50%}
    98	  .dot{position:relative;width:12px;height:12px;border-radius:50%;align-self:center;
    99	    background:var(--bg-0);border:2px solid currentColor}
   100	  /* A BROKEN rail is the drift signal: the line itself is severed, so the
   101	     path visibly does not continue past a stage with no evidence. */
   102	  .stage.broken .rail::before{background:repeating-linear-gradient(
   103	      to bottom,var(--fail) 0 4px,transparent 4px 8px)}
   104	  /* Both are spans inside a <button> (a button may not contain block-level
   105	     children), so they need an explicit display:block or the label and its
   106	     reason render as one run-on sentence. */
   107	  .stage .label{display:block;font-weight:500}
   108	  .stage .why{display:block;color:var(--text-2);font-size:12px;margin-top:2px}
   109	  .row .name,.row .meta{display:block}
   110	
   111	  /* Motion is bound to state, never to time. Only a genuinely ACTIVE stage
   112	     pulses, and nothing else in this file moves at all. */
   113	  @keyframes pulse{0%,100%{opacity:1}50%{opacity:.45}}
   114	  .stage.is-active .dot{animation:pulse 2s ease-in-out infinite}
   115	  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce){
   116	    .stage.is-active .dot{animation:none;box-shadow:0 0 0 3px color-mix(in srgb,var(--active) 35%,transparent)}
   117	    *{transition:none!important}
   118	  }
   119	
   120	  /* ── lists ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   121	  .rows{list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0;display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:calc(var(--sp)*2)}
   122	  .row{display:flex;justify-content:space-between;gap:calc(var(--sp)*3);align-items:flex-start;
   123	    padding:calc(var(--sp)*2) 0;border-bottom:1px solid var(--line)}
   124	  .row:last-child{border-bottom:0}
   125	  .row .name{font-weight:500}
   126	  .row .meta{color:var(--text-2);font-size:12px;margin-top:2px}
   127	  .badges{display:flex;gap:calc(var(--sp)*1.5);flex-wrap:wrap;justify-content:flex-end}
   128	
   129	  /* ── inspector ──────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   130	  .kv{display:grid;grid-template-columns:auto minmax(0,1fr);gap:calc(var(--sp)*2) calc(var(--sp)*3);font-size:13px}
   131	  .kv dt{color:var(--text-2)}
   132	  .kv dd{margin:0;word-break:break-word}
   133	  .prov{margin-top:calc(var(--sp)*3);padding-top:calc(var(--sp)*3);border-top:1px solid var(--line);
   134	    color:var(--text-2);font-size:11px}
   135	  .prov code{color:var(--text-1)}
   136	  .empty{color:var(--text-2);font-size:13px}
   137	
   138	  .note{margin:calc(var(--sp)*3) 0 0;padding:calc(var(--sp)*3);background:var(--bg-2);
   139	    border-left:2px solid var(--warn);border-radius:0 var(--radius) var(--radius) 0;
   140	    color:var(--text-1);font-size:12px}
   141	  .fatal{border-left-color:var(--fail)}
   142	
   143	  .sr{position:absolute;width:1px;height:1px;overflow:hidden;clip:rect(0 0 0 0);white-space:nowrap}
   144	
   145	  /* ── responsive (§M) ────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   146	  @media (max-width:1279px){ main{grid-template-columns:minmax(0,1fr)} }
   147	  @media (max-width:719px){
   148	    main{padding:calc(var(--sp)*3);gap:calc(var(--sp)*3)}

   151	    .stage .badges{grid-area:badge;justify-content:flex-start}
