   668	function cmdValidateReview(args) {
   669	  const files = args.review.length ? args.review : args._;
   670	  if (!files.length) die('usage: --validate-review <review.json> [more.json ...]', EXIT_USAGE);
   671	  let bad = 0;
   672	  const results = files.map(loadReview);
   673	  if (args.json) {
   674	    out(JSON.stringify(results.map((r) => ({ path: r.path, ok: r.ok, errors: r.errors })), null, 2));
   675	    return results.every((r) => r.ok) ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_USAGE;
   676	  }
   677	  out('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEW RECORD VALIDATION');
   678	  out('='.repeat(52));
   679	  for (const r of results) {
   680	    if (r.ok) { out(`ok    ${r.path}  (${r.rec.reviewer} / ${r.rec.disposition})`); }
   681	    else { bad++; out(`FAIL  ${r.path}`); for (const e of r.errors) out(`        ${e}`); }
   682	  }
   683	  out('');
   684	  out(bad ? `${bad} invalid record(s).` : `All ${results.length} record(s) valid.`);
   685	  return bad ? EXIT_USAGE : EXIT_PASS;
   686	}
   687	
   688	// Gather review records from explicit --review args and from the conventional
   689	// evidence directory, so CI does not have to enumerate them.
   690	function collectReviewPaths(args) {
   691	  const explicit = args.review.slice();
   692	  const dir = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
   693	  // Only the top level of reviews/ is evidence. Subdirectories are archives —
   694	  // notably reviews/legacy-unattested/, which holds records produced before
   695	  // attestation existed. Those are history and must not be able to approve or
   696	  // block anything; signing them retroactively would be the builder minting
   697	  // approvals for reviews it did not produce.
   698	  const fromDir = fs.existsSync(dir)
   699	    ? fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })
   700	        .filter((d) => d.isFile() && d.name.endsWith('.json'))
   701	        .map((d) => path.join(dir, d.name))
   702	    : [];
   703	  return [...new Set([...explicit, ...fromDir])];
   704	}
   705	
   706	// ── COMMAND: --gate-done ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   707	// The fail-closed question: given what actually exists on disk, has this change
   708	// earned the right to move forward?
   709	//
   710	// Terminal states are deliberately NOT "DONE". Reviews existing is not the same
   711	// as software being correct, and a word that implies finished invites everyone
   712	// downstream to stop checking.
   713	const STATE_READY_DET = 'READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION';
   714	const STATE_READY_PR = 'READY_FOR_PR';
   715	
   716	function gateDone(args) {
   717	  const problems = [];
   718	  const observed = [];
   719	
   720	  // 1. Subject binding is MANDATORY and always enforced. Without it there is
   721	  //    no answer to "reviewed WHAT?", and every downstream check is decoration.
   722	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
   723	  const claimed = args.subjectSha || args.diffSha || null;
   724	  if (!claimed) {
   725	    problems.push({
   726	      rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING',
   727	      detail: 'no --subject-sha was supplied. A gate decision with no bound subject certifies nothing. Compute it with `--subject --json` and pass it back.',
   728	    });
   729	  } else if (!/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(claimed)) {
   730	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING', detail: `--subject-sha ${JSON.stringify(claimed)} is not a sha256 hex digest` });
   731	  } else if (claimed !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   732	    problems.push({
   733	      rule: 'ENGOS-SUBJECT-MISMATCH',
   734	      detail: `--subject-sha ${claimed.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the subject computed here (${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…). The tree moved, or the wrong subject was supplied.`,
   735	    });
   736	  } else {
   737	    observed.push(`subject bound: ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s)`);
   738	  }
   739	  if (subject.excludedAsEvidence.length) {
   740	    observed.push(`${subject.excludedAsEvidence.length} evidence path(s) excluded from the subject — adding review records does not move this hash`);
   741	  }
   742	
   743	  // 2. Lane, computed from the SUBJECT only.
   744	  const cls = classify(subject.subjectPaths, { milestone: args.milestone, novel: args.novel, diffLines: args.diffLines });
   745	  observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? ' (high-risk)' : ''}; required reviewers: ${cls.requiredReviewers.join(', ') || 'none'}`);
   746	
   747	  // 3. Packet: required for every lane except LIGHT.
   748	  let packetId = null;
   749	  if (cls.requiresPacket) {
   750	    if (!args.packet) {
   751	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `lane ${cls.lane} requires a task packet; none was supplied` });
   752	    } else if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) {
   753	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `packet not found: ${args.packet}` });
   754	    } else {
   755	      const pv = spawnSync('node', [PACKET_TOOLS, '--validate', args.packet], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   756	      if (pv.status !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-INVALID', detail: `packet-tools rejected the packet (exit ${pv.status}). Fix the packet first.` });
   757	      else observed.push('task packet is valid (packet-tools.cjs)');
   758	      try { packetId = readJSON(args.packet).packetId; } catch { /* reported above */ }
   759	
   760	      // CONFIRMED FINDING #3: the gate validated the packet's SHAPE but never
   761	      // checked that what actually changed was inside filesAllowed. A packet
   762	      // could authorize five paths while the diff touched fifty, and the gate
   763	      // reported PASS. Authorization is only meaningful if it is compared
   764	      // against the work.
   765	      try {
   766	        const pk = readJSON(args.packet);
   767	        const allowed = pk.filesAllowed || [];
   768	        const outside = subject.subjectPaths.filter((sp2) => !allowed.some((g) => globToRe(g).test(sp2)));
   769	        if (outside.length) {
   770	          problems.push({
   771	            rule: 'ENGOS-PATH-UNAUTHORIZED',
   772	            detail: `${outside.length} changed path(s) are outside the packet's filesAllowed: ${outside.slice(0, 6).join(', ')}${outside.length > 6 ? ' …' : ''}. The packet did not authorize this work.`,
   773	          });
   774	        } else if (subject.subjectPaths.length) {
   775	          observed.push(`all ${subject.subjectPaths.length} subject path(s) are inside the packet's filesAllowed`);
   776	        }
   777	      } catch (e) {
   778	        problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PATH-UNAUTHORIZED', detail: `could not read filesAllowed from the packet: ${e.message}` });
   779	      }
   780	
   781	      const sp = specCheck(args.packet);
   782	      if (!sp.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED', detail: `${sp.unresolved.length} source(s) of truth are neither pinnable nor marked UNVERIFIED: ${sp.unresolved.map((r) => r.source).join(', ')}` });
   783	      else observed.push(`spec pin: ${sp.rows.length - sp.unverified.length} pinned, ${sp.unverified.length} explicitly UNVERIFIED`);
   784	      var specRows = sp;
   785	    }
   786	  } else {
   787	    observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}: no task packet required — deterministic checks are the whole gate here`);
   788	  }
   789	
   790	  // 4. Load records. Malformed records are errors; they are never skipped.
   791	  const loaded = collectReviewPaths(args).map((rp) => loadReview(rp, { packetPath: args.packet }));
   792	  for (const l of loaded) if (!l.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MALFORMED', detail: `${l.path}: ${l.errors.join('; ')}` });
   793	  const allValid = loaded.filter((l) => l.ok).map((l) => l.rec);
   794	
   795	  // 5. Partition by subject hash BEFORE anything else looks at findings.
   796	  //    A record about a different subject is not "stale evidence to weigh" —
   797	  //    it is evidence about a different thing, and it must not contaminate
   798	  //    this decision in either direction. It cannot approve this change and
   799	  //    its findings cannot block it.
   800	  const bound = [];
   801	  const foreign = [];
   802	  for (const rec of allValid) {
   803	    const sha = rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.diffSha256;
   804	    if (sha === subject.subjectSha256) bound.push(rec);
   805	    else foreign.push(rec);
   806	  }
   807	  if (foreign.length) {
   808	    observed.push(`${foreign.length} review record(s) bound to a different subject were IGNORED (they neither approve nor block this change)`);
   809	  }
   810	
   811	  // 6. Supersession — the one documented deterministic replacement rule.
   812	  //    A record may declare `supersedes: <reviewId>`; the named record is
   813	  //    dropped. Anything else that leaves two records for the same reviewer on
   814	  //    the same subject is AMBIGUOUS and blocks, because picking a winner by
   815	  //    timestamp or file order would let whichever record happened to sort
   816	  //    later silently overrule a rejection.
   817	  // CONFIRMED FINDING #4: supersedes was a global Set, so a record from ANY
   818	  // reviewer could retire a record from any other — grok could delete codex's
   819	  // REJECT simply by naming its reviewId. Supersession is a reviewer revising
   820	  // its own verdict, so it is now valid only within the same reviewer, the same
   821	  // subject, and the same packet. Anything else is refused rather than silently
   822	  // honoured.
   823	  const superseded = new Set();
   824	  const byId = new Map(bound.map((r) => [r.reviewId, r]));
   825	  for (const rec of bound) {
   826	    if (!rec.supersedes) continue;
   827	    if (rec.supersedes === rec.reviewId) {
   828	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-SUPERSEDE-SELF', detail: `${rec.reviewId} declares it supersedes itself.` });
   829	      continue;
   830	    }
   831	    const target = byId.get(rec.supersedes);
   832	    if (!target) {
   833	      problems.push({
   834	        rule: 'ENGOS-SUPERSEDE-UNKNOWN',
   835	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} claims to supersede "${rec.supersedes}", which is not an active record for this subject. A supersession that names nothing retires nothing.`,
   836	      });
   837	      continue;
   838	    }
   839	    if (target.reviewer !== rec.reviewer) {
   840	      problems.push({
   841	        rule: 'ENGOS-SUPERSEDE-CROSS-REVIEWER',
   842	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) attempts to supersede ${target.reviewId} (${target.reviewer}). A reviewer may revise only its OWN verdict — otherwise any reviewer could retire another's rejection.`,
   843	      });
   844	      continue;
   845	    }
   846	    if (target.packetId !== rec.packetId) {
   847	      problems.push({
   848	        rule: 'ENGOS-SUPERSEDE-CROSS-PACKET',
   849	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} supersedes a record bound to a different packet (${target.packetId} vs ${rec.packetId}).`,
   850	      });
   851	      continue;
   852	    }
   853	    superseded.add(rec.supersedes);
   854	  }
   855	  const active = bound.filter((r) => !superseded.has(r.reviewId));
   856	  if (superseded.size) observed.push(`${superseded.size} record(s) superseded by an explicit supersedes declaration`);
   857	
   858	  const byReviewer = {};
   859	  for (const rec of active) (byReviewer[rec.reviewer] ||= []).push(rec);
   860	  for (const [who, recs] of Object.entries(byReviewer)) {
   861	    if (recs.length > 1) {
   862	      problems.push({
   863	        rule: 'ENGOS-AMBIGUOUS-REVIEWS',
   864	        detail: `${recs.length} active records from "${who}" for this subject (${recs.map((r) => `${r.reviewId}:${r.disposition}`).join(', ')}). Exactly one verdict per reviewer per subject. Resolve by adding "supersedes" to the record that replaces the other.`,
   865	      });
   866	    }
   867	  }
   868	
   869	  // 7. Packet binding of records.
   870	  if (packetId) {
   871	    for (const rec of active) {
   872	      if (rec.packetId !== packetId) {
   873	        problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-WRONG-PACKET', detail: `${rec.reviewId} is bound to packet ${rec.packetId}, not ${packetId}` });
   874	      }
   875	    }
   876	  }
   877	
   878	  // 8. Coverage: a record must have read the WHOLE subject. Subset coverage is
   879	  //    the quiet failure — a reviewer that saw 3 of 11 files reporting APPROVE
   880	  //    looks identical to one that saw everything.
   881	  const subjectSet = new Set(subject.subjectPaths);
   882	  for (const rec of active) {
   883	    if (rec.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE') continue;
   884	    const seen = new Set((rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.changedPaths) || []);
   885	    const missing = [...subjectSet].filter((p) => !seen.has(p));
   886	    const extra = [...seen].filter((p) => !subjectSet.has(p));
   887	    if (missing.length) {
   888	      problems.push({
   889	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-SHORT',
   890	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) did not cover ${missing.length} subject path(s): ${missing.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}${missing.length > 5 ? ' …' : ''}. Partial coverage is not approval of the whole change.`,
   891	      });
   892	    }
   893	    if (extra.length) {
   894	      problems.push({
   895	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-EXTRA',
   896	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) claims to have reviewed ${extra.length} path(s) that are not in the subject: ${extra.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}. The record does not describe this change.`,
   897	      });
   898	    }
   899	  }
   900	
   901	  // 9. Every required reviewer must have an APPROVE on this exact subject.
   902	  if (byReviewer['claude-self']) {
   903	    observed.push('claude-self record present (recorded for traceability; it satisfies no required slot)');
   904	  }
   905	  for (const need of cls.requiredReviewers) {
   906	    const recs = byReviewer[need] || [];
   907	    if (!recs.length) {
   908	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING', detail: `${need} is required for lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? '/high-risk' : ''} and has no valid review record bound to this subject. Missing evidence is a BLOCK, never an implicit pass.` });
   909	      continue;
   910	    }
   911	    const unavailable = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   912	    if (unavailable) {
   913	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${need} reported UNAVAILABLE: ${unavailable.unavailableReason}. Reported honestly — and it still blocks, because this lane requires that review.` });
   914	      continue;
   915	    }
   916	    const rejected = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT');
   917	    if (rejected) { problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED', detail: `${need} returned REJECT (${rejected.reviewId})` }); continue; }
   918	    observed.push(`${need}: ${recs.map((r) => r.disposition).join(', ')}`);
   919	  }
   920	
   921	  // 10. Blocking findings — from ACTIVE, SUBJECT-BOUND records only.
   922	  const blocking = [];
   923	  for (const rec of active) {
   924	    for (const f of rec.findings || []) {
   925	      if ((f.severity === 'CRITICAL' || f.severity === 'HIGH') && f.status === 'OPEN') {
   926	        blocking.push({ reviewer: rec.reviewer, severity: f.severity, file: f.file || '(unspecified)', problem: f.problem });
   927	      }
   928	      // A FIXED finding must name a re-verifying record that is itself active
   929	      // and bound to this subject, and that reviewer may not be the builder.
   930	      if (f.status === 'FIXED') {
   931	        const v = active.find((r) => r.reviewId === f.verifiedByReviewId);
   932	        if (!v) {
   933	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-UNVERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED cites verifiedByReviewId "${f.verifiedByReviewId}", which is not an active review record for this subject.` });
   934	        } else if (v.reviewer === 'claude-self') {
   935	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-SELF-VERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED is verified only by claude-self (${v.reviewId}). The builder cannot verify its own fix.` });
   936	        }
   937	      }
   938	    }
   939	  }
   940	  for (const b of blocking) {
   941	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-OPEN-BLOCKING-FINDING', detail: `${b.severity} from ${b.reviewer} in ${b.file}: ${b.problem}` });
   942	  }
   943	
   944	  // 11. Unverified items surfaced, never dropped.
   945	  const unverified = [];
   946	  for (const rec of active) for (const u of rec.unverified || []) unverified.push(`${rec.reviewer}: ${u}`);
   947	  if (typeof specRows !== 'undefined' && specRows) for (const r of specRows.unverified) unverified.push(`spec: ${r.source}`);
   948	
   949	  // 12. Deterministic checks. READY_FOR_PR is only reachable when this process
   950	  //     actually executed them — not when a caller asserts they passed.
   951	  let checks = null;
   952	  if (args.runChecks) {
   953	    if (!args.packet) {
   954	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-CHECKS-NO-PACKET', detail: '--run-checks needs a packet to read testsRequired from' });
   955	    } else {
   956	      checks = runDeterministicChecks(args.packet);
   957	      for (const c of checks) if (c.exit !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED', detail: `${c.cmd} exited ${c.exit}` });
   958	      observed.push(`deterministic checks executed here: ${checks.filter((c) => c.exit === 0).length}/${checks.length} passed`);
   959	    }
   960	  }
   961	
   962	  const ok = problems.length === 0;
   963	  const state = !ok ? 'BLOCKED' : (checks ? STATE_READY_PR : STATE_READY_DET);
   964	
   965	  return { ok, state, problems, observed, unverified, classification: cls, subject, reviewsBound: bound.length, reviewsActive: active.length, reviewsForeign: foreign.length, checks };
   966	}
   967	
   968	// Execute the packet's declared testsRequired. These are command strings from a
   969	// packet that has already passed packet-tools validation and the Builder
   970	// Control gate; they are run through `bash -lc` because that is what they are —
   971	// commands. No caller-supplied data is interpolated into them.
   972	function runDeterministicChecks(packetPath) {
   973	  let pkt;
   974	  try { pkt = readJSON(packetPath); } catch { return []; }
   975	  const cmds = (pkt.testsRequired || []).filter((c) => !/--gate-done/.test(c));
   976	  const results = [];
   977	  for (const cmd of cmds) {
   978	    const r = spawnSync('bash', ['-lc', cmd], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   979	    results.push({ cmd, exit: r.status === null ? 1 : r.status, tail: ((r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr || '')).trim().split('\n').slice(-3).join('\n') });
   980	  }
   981	  return results;
   982	}
   983	
   984	function cmdGateDone(args) {
   985	  const r = gateDone(args);
   986	  if (args.json) { out(JSON.stringify(r, null, 2)); return r.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_BLOCK; }
   987	
   988	  out('ENGINEERING OS — GATE');
   989	  out('='.repeat(52));
   990	  out(`lane            : ${r.classification.lane}${r.classification.highRisk ? ' (HIGH-RISK)' : ''}`);
   991	  out(`subject         : ${r.subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… (${r.subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s))`);
   992	  out(`required        : ${r.classification.requiredReviewers.join(' + ') || 'none — deterministic checks only'}`);
   993	  out(`review records  : ${r.reviewsActive} active / ${r.reviewsBound} bound / ${r.reviewsForeign} ignored (other subject)`);
   994	  out('');
   995	  out('OBSERVED:');
   996	  for (const o of r.observed) out(`  - ${o}`);
   997	  if (r.checks) {
   998	    out('');
   999	    out('DETERMINISTIC CHECKS (executed by this process):');
  1000	    for (const c of r.checks) out(`  exit ${String(c.exit).padEnd(3)} ${c.cmd}`);
  1001	  }
  1002	  if (r.unverified.length) {
  1003	    out('');
  1004	    out('UNVERIFIED (not failures — things nobody actually checked):');
  1005	    for (const u of r.unverified) out(`  - ${u}`);
  1006	  }
  1007	  out('');
  1008	  if (!r.ok) {
  1009	    out('RESULT: BLOCKED');
  1010	    out('');
  1011	    for (const p of r.problems) { out(`  rule   : ${p.rule}`); out(`  reason : ${p.detail}`); out(''); }
  1012	    out('No override flag exists here by design. Resolve the rule, or amend the');
  1013	    out('packet authorization — which itself leaves a ledger trail.');
  1014	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
  1015	  }
  1016	  out(`RESULT: ${r.state}`);
  1017	  out('');
  1018	  if (r.state === STATE_READY_DET) {
  1019	    out('Required review evidence is present and bound to this subject.');
  1020	    out('Deterministic checks were NOT run by this process — run them, or use');
  1021	    out('--run-checks, before treating this as ready for a pull request.');
  1022	  } else {
  1023	    out('Required review evidence is present and bound to this subject, and the');
  1024	    out("packet's deterministic checks were executed here and passed.");
  1025	  }
  1026	  out('');
  1027	  out('This is NOT a claim that the software is correct, and NOT a runtime or');
  1028	  out('deploy verdict. Runtime validation is a separate step that has not run.');
  1029	  return EXIT_PASS;
  1030	}
  1031	// ── ledger append (reuses the ONE ledger) ───────────────────────────────────
  1032	function appendLedger(entry) {
  1033	  const tmp = path.join(require('os').tmpdir(), `engos-ledger-${process.pid}.json`);
  1034	  fs.writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(entry, null, 2));
  1035	  const r = spawnSync('node', [LEDGER_WRITER, '--append', tmp], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
  1036	  try { fs.unlinkSync(tmp); } catch { /* best effort */ }
  1037	  return r;
  1038	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * ledger-writer.cjs  — Builder Control System v1, Module 3
     4	 *
     5	 * Usage:
     6	 *   --append <entry.json>          Validate entry against ledger-entry.schema.json,
     7	 *                                  then append it to builder-control/ledger.json.
     8	 *                                  NEVER mutates or truncates existing entries.
     9	 *   --migrate-policy-ledger        Migrate the single policy/ledger.json record into
    10	 *                                  builder-control/ledger.json as a back-dated IMPORTED
    11	 *                                  entry. Leaves policy/ledger.json as a stub.
    12	 *                                  Safe to re-run: idempotent (checks if already migrated).
    13	 *
    14	 * Invariant: the ledger file is a JSON array. Every operation that touches the file
    15	 * reads the current array, validates no mutation occurred, then writes
    16	 * [  ...existingEntries, newEntry  ]. It never truncates.
    17	 *
    18	 * No npm deps — only Node built-ins + shared hand-rolled JSON-Schema validator.
    19	 */
    20	'use strict';
    21	
    22	const fs   = require('fs');
    23	const path = require('path');
    24	const os   = require('os');
    25	
    26	// ─── Paths ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27	// The canonical ledger. Tests need an ISOLATED ledger — asserting absolute
    28	// counts against the shared production file is what made ledger-atomicity fail
    29	// under a parallel runner, and its restore-on-exit was destroying other suites'
    30	// legitimately written entries.
    31	//
    32	// The override is deliberately CONSTRAINED to the OS temp directory. Allowing an
    33	// arbitrary path would turn this into an evidence-hiding switch: point the
    34	// ledger somewhere else and gate decisions stop being recorded where anyone
    35	// looks. Confining it to a temp dir makes it useful for isolation and useless
    36	// for redirecting production evidence into another repository path.
    37	function resolveLedgerFile() {
    38	  const override = process.env.AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE;
    39	  if (!override) return path.join(__dirname, 'ledger.json');
    40	  const abs = path.resolve(override);
    41	  const tmpRoot = fs.realpathSync(os.tmpdir());
    42	  let real;
    43	  try { real = fs.realpathSync(path.dirname(abs)); } catch { real = path.dirname(abs); }
    44	  if (real !== tmpRoot && !real.startsWith(tmpRoot + path.sep)) {
    45	    throw new Error(
    46	      `AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE must point inside ${tmpRoot} (got ${abs}). ` +
    47	      'Redirecting the canonical ledger outside a temp directory would hide gate decisions.'
    48	    );
    49	  }
    50	  return abs;
    51	}
    52	const LEDGER_FILE   = resolveLedgerFile();
    53	const SCHEMA_FILE   = path.join(__dirname, 'schemas', 'ledger-entry.schema.json');
    54	const POLICY_LEDGER = path.join(__dirname, '..', 'policy', 'ledger.json');
    55	
    56	// ─── Hand-rolled JSON-Schema draft-07 validator (same logic as packet-tools) ─
    57	function validateSchema(schema, data, ctx, defs) {
    58	  ctx  = ctx  || '#';
    59	  defs = defs || schema['$defs'] || schema['definitions'] || {};
    60	
    61	  const errors = [];
    62	
    63	  if (schema['$ref']) {
    64	    const ref = schema['$ref'];
    65	    if (ref.startsWith('#/$defs/')) {
    66	      const defKey = ref.slice('#/$defs/'.length);
    67	      if (!defs[defKey]) { errors.push(`${ctx}: unresolved $ref ${ref}`); return errors; }
    68	      return validateSchema(defs[defKey], data, ctx, defs);
    69	    }
    70	    errors.push(`${ctx}: unsupported $ref ${ref}`);
    71	    return errors;
    72	  }
    73	
    74	  if (schema.type) {
    75	    const types = Array.isArray(schema.type) ? schema.type : [schema.type];
    76	    const actual = data === null ? 'null' : Array.isArray(data) ? 'array' : typeof data;
    77	    // JSON-Schema: 'integer' means a number with no fractional part
    78	    const typesForCheck = types.map(t => t === 'integer' ? 'number' : t);
    79	    if (!typesForCheck.includes(actual)) {
    80	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected type ${types.join('|')} but got ${actual}`);
    81	      return errors;
    82	    }
    83	    // If type is 'integer', also verify it's a whole number
    84	    if (types.includes('integer') && actual === 'number' && !Number.isInteger(data)) {
    85	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected integer but got non-integer number ${data}`);
    86	      return errors;
    87	    }
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  if (schema.required && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    91	    for (const req of schema.required) {
    92	      if (!(req in data)) errors.push(`${ctx}: missing required property "${req}"`);
    93	    }
    94	  }
    95	
    96	  if (schema.properties && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    97	    for (const [key, subSchema] of Object.entries(schema.properties)) {
    98	      if (key in data) {
    99	        errors.push(...validateSchema(subSchema, data[key], `${ctx}/${key}`, defs));
   100	      }
   101	    }
   102	  }
   103	
   104	  if (schema.additionalProperties === false && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
   105	    const allowed = new Set(Object.keys(schema.properties || {}));
   106	    for (const key of Object.keys(data)) {
   107	      if (!allowed.has(key)) errors.push(`${ctx}: unexpected additional property "${key}"`);
   108	    }
   109	  }
   110	
   111	  if (schema.pattern && typeof data === 'string') {
   112	    if (!new RegExp(schema.pattern).test(data)) {
   113	      errors.push(`${ctx}: string does not match pattern ${schema.pattern}`);
   114	    }
   115	  }
   116	
   117	  if (schema.enum && !schema.enum.includes(data)) {
   118	    errors.push(`${ctx}: must be one of ${JSON.stringify(schema.enum)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  if (typeof schema.minItems === 'number' && Array.isArray(data) && data.length < schema.minItems) {
   122	    errors.push(`${ctx}: array has ${data.length} items, minimum is ${schema.minItems}`);
   123	  }
   124	
   125	  if (schema.items && Array.isArray(data)) {
   126	    data.forEach((item, idx) => {
   127	      errors.push(...validateSchema(schema.items, item, `${ctx}[${idx}]`, defs));
   128	    });
   129	  }
   130	
   131	  if ('const' in schema && data !== schema.const) {
   132	    errors.push(`${ctx}: expected const ${JSON.stringify(schema.const)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
   133	  }
   134	
   135	  return errors;
   136	}
   137	
   138	// ─── Load JSON file ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   139	function loadJSON(filePath) {
   140	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8'));
   141	}
   142	
   143	// ─── Read the current ledger array (creates empty array if file absent) ────────
   144	function readLedger() {
   145	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) return [];
   146	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, 'utf8').trim();
   147	  if (!raw) return [];
   148	  const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
   149	  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {
   150	    throw new Error(`Ledger at ${LEDGER_FILE} is not a JSON array — aborting to prevent data loss`);
   151	  }
   152	  return parsed;
   153	}
   154	
   155	// ─── Atomic, idempotent ledger append ────────────────────────────────────────
   156	// CONFIRMED FINDING #7 (CRITICAL): the previous writeLedger() built
   157	// [...oldEntries, newEntry] from an in-memory snapshot, then checked only that
   158	// the on-disk array was not SHORTER than that snapshot. A concurrent append
   159	// made the file longer, the check passed, and the stale array was written over
   160	// the top — silently destroying the other process's entry. In an append-only
   161	// evidence ledger, a lost entry is a lost gate decision.
   162	//
   163	// The repair has three parts, each closing a distinct hole:
   164	//   1. an exclusive lock, so two appends cannot interleave at all;
   165	//   2. re-reading the CURRENT file under that lock, so the base is never stale;
   166	//   3. write-temp-then-rename, so a crash mid-write cannot leave a half-file.
   167	//
   168	// Idempotency is separate: a repeated operationId is a NO-OP rather than a
   169	// second entry, which is what makes an external retry safe instead of
   170	// duplicating the effect it is retrying.
   171	
   172	const LOCK_FILE = LEDGER_FILE + '.lock';
   173	const LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000;
   174	const LOCK_STALE_MS = 60000;
   175	
   176	function acquireLock() {
   177	  const deadline = Date.now() + LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS;
   178	  for (;;) {
   179	    try {
   180	      const fd = fs.openSync(LOCK_FILE, 'wx');
   181	      fs.writeFileSync(fd, String(process.pid));
   182	      return fd;
   183	    } catch (e) {
   184	      if (e.code !== 'EEXIST') throw e;
   185	      // A lock left behind by a killed process must not wedge the system
   186	      // forever, but "stale" has to be generous enough that a slow-but-alive
   187	      // writer is never stolen from.
   188	      try {
   189	        const age = Date.now() - fs.statSync(LOCK_FILE).mtimeMs;
   190	        if (age > LOCK_STALE_MS) { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); continue; }
   191	      } catch { /* raced with the holder releasing it */ }
   192	      if (Date.now() > deadline) {
   193	        throw new Error(`could not acquire the ledger lock within ${LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS}ms (held by another process). Refusing to write rather than risk clobbering it.`);
   194	      }
   195	      // Busy-wait briefly. Deliberately synchronous: this whole module is a
   196	      // synchronous CLI, and an async lock here would change its contract.
   197	      const until = Date.now() + 25;
   198	      while (Date.now() < until) { /* spin */ }
   199	    }
   200	  }
   201	}
   202	
   203	function releaseLock(fd) {
   204	  try { fs.closeSync(fd); } catch { /* already closed */ }
   205	  try { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); } catch { /* already gone */ }
   206	}
   207	
   208	// Append under an exclusive lock. Returns {appended, length, duplicate}.
   209	function appendAtomic(newEntry) {
   210	  const fd = acquireLock();
   211	  try {
   212	    // Re-read INSIDE the lock. This is the fix: the base is whatever is on
   213	    // disk right now, never a snapshot taken before the lock was held.
   214	    const current = fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE) ? readLedger() : [];
   215	
   216	    if (newEntry.operationId) {
   217	      const existing = current.find((e) => e && e.operationId === newEntry.operationId);
   218	      if (existing) {
   219	        return { appended: false, duplicate: true, length: current.length, existingEntryId: existing.entryId };
   220	      }
   221	    }
   222	    if (newEntry.entryId && current.some((e) => e && e.entryId === newEntry.entryId)) {
   223	      throw new Error(`entryId "${newEntry.entryId}" already exists in the ledger. Entry ids must be unique.`);
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    const next = [...current, newEntry];
   227	    const tmp = LEDGER_FILE + '.tmp-' + process.pid;
   228	    fs.writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(next, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   229	    fs.renameSync(tmp, LEDGER_FILE); // atomic within a filesystem
   230	    return { appended: true, duplicate: false, length: next.length };
   231	  } finally {
   232	    releaseLock(fd);
   233	  }
   234	}
   235	
   236	// Retained for callers that still expect the old name. It now delegates to the
   237	// atomic path and ignores the caller's stale snapshot entirely, because using
   238	// that snapshot as the write base was the defect.
   239	function writeLedger(_oldEntriesIgnored, newEntry) {
   240	  return appendAtomic(newEntry).length;
   241	}
   242	
   243	// ─── Validate an entry object against the ledger-entry schema ─────────────────
   244	function validateEntry(entry) {
   245	  const schema = loadJSON(SCHEMA_FILE);
   246	  const defs   = schema['$defs'] || {};
   247	  return validateSchema(schema, entry, '#', defs);
   248	}
   249	
   250	// ─── --append <entry.json> ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   251	function appendEntry(entryPath) {
   252	  // Load entry
   253	  let entry;
   254	  try {
   255	    entry = loadJSON(path.resolve(entryPath));
   256	  } catch (e) {
   257	    console.error(`ERROR loading entry file: ${e.message}`);
   258	    process.exit(2);
   259	  }
   260	
   261	  // Validate
   262	  const errors = validateEntry(entry);
   263	  if (errors.length) {
   264	    console.error('SCHEMA VALIDATION FAILED for entry:');
   265	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   266	    process.exit(1);
   267	  }
   268	
   269	  // Append atomically. A repeated operationId is reported as a no-op rather
   270	  // than written twice — the caller needs to know its retry was absorbed.
   271	  let res;
   272	  try {
   273	    res = appendAtomic(entry);
   274	  } catch (e) {
   275	    console.error(`[ledger-writer] REFUSED: ${e.message}`);
   276	    process.exit(1);
   277	  }
   278	  if (res.duplicate) {
   279	    console.log(`[ledger-writer] NO-OP: operationId "${entry.operationId}" is already recorded as "${res.existingEntryId}".`);
   280	    console.log('[ledger-writer] The retry was absorbed; no duplicate effect was written.');
   281	    process.exit(0);
   282	  }
   283	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Appended entry "${entry.entryId}" to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   284	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${res.length - 1} → ${res.length}  (grew by 1)`);
   285	  process.exit(0);
   286	}
   287	
   288	// ─── --migrate-policy-ledger ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   289	function migratePolicyLedger() {
   290	  // 1. Read the policy ledger
   291	  if (!fs.existsSync(POLICY_LEDGER)) {
   292	    console.error(`ERROR: policy/ledger.json not found at ${POLICY_LEDGER}`);
   293	    process.exit(2);
   294	  }
   295	
   296	  let source;
   297	  try {
   298	    source = loadJSON(POLICY_LEDGER);
   299	  } catch (e) {
   300	    console.error(`ERROR parsing policy/ledger.json: ${e.message}`);
   301	    process.exit(2);
   302	  }
   303	
   304	  // Check if already a stub (migration already done)
   305	  if (source && source._stub === true) {
   306	    console.log('[ledger-writer] policy/ledger.json is already a stub — migration already complete.');
   307	    process.exit(0);
   308	  }
   309	
   310	  // 2. Extract the single record
   311	  // Shape: { "agent:main:main": { artifacts: { research_report: [ { ts, source: { via, targetAgent, targetRole } } ] } } }
   312	  const agentKey   = 'agent:main:main';
   313	  const agentBlock = source[agentKey];
   314	
   315	  if (!agentBlock) {
   316	    console.error(`ERROR: Expected key "${agentKey}" in policy/ledger.json — STOP (stopCondition: migrated record would lose data)`);
   317	    process.exit(2);
   318	  }
   319	
   320	  const artifacts = agentBlock.artifacts || {};
   321	  const reports   = artifacts.research_report || [];
   322	
   323	  if (reports.length === 0) {
   324	    console.error('ERROR: No research_report entries found — STOP (would lose data)');
   325	    process.exit(2);
   326	  }
   327	
   328	  // We migrate ONE record as specified (the contract says "the single existing record")
   329	  const original = reports[0];
   330	  const via          = original.source && original.source.via         ? original.source.via         : null;
   331	  const targetAgent  = original.source && original.source.targetAgent ? original.source.targetAgent : null;
   332	  const targetRole   = original.source && original.source.targetRole  ? original.source.targetRole  : null;
   333	  const origTs       = original.ts || '2026-04-16T14:02:52.750Z';
   334	
   335	  // 3. Check if already migrated in ledger (idempotency)
   336	  const existing = readLedger();
   337	  const alreadyMigrated = existing.some(e => e.gate === 'legacy-import' && e.notes && e.notes.includes('agent:main:main'));
   338	  if (alreadyMigrated) {
   339	    console.log('[ledger-writer] Entry for agent:main:main already present in ledger — skipping duplicate migration.');
   340	    process.exit(0);
   341	  }
   342	
   343	  // 4. Build the IMPORTED ledger entry
   344	  // Preserve ALL original fields in notes so no data is lost.
   345	  const preservedOriginal = JSON.stringify(original);
   346	
   347	  const importedEntry = {
   348	    entryId:       'LED-LEGACY-IMPORT-0001',
   349	    ts:            new Date().toISOString(),               // migration timestamp (now)
   350	    packetId:      null,                                   // no packet existed at original write time
   351	    agentId:       targetAgent || 'daniel',               // the original target agent
   352	    gate:          'legacy-import',
   353	    status:        'IMPORTED',
   354	    blockRule:     null,
   355	    operation:     `research_report dispatch via ${via || 'sessions_spawn'} to ${targetAgent || 'daniel'} (back-dated ${origTs})`,
   356	    changed:       [],
   357	    commandsRun:   [],
   358	    testsRun:      [],
   359	    screenshots:   [],
   360	    commitSha:     null,
   361	    bundleHash:    null,
   362	    evidencePaths: [],
   363	    driftChecks:   [],
   364	    notes: [
   365	      `MIGRATED from policy/ledger.json on ${new Date().toISOString()}.`,
   366	      `Original key: agent:main:main`,
   367	      `Original ts: ${origTs}`,
   368	      `Original via: ${via}`,
   369	      `Original targetAgent: ${targetAgent}`,
   370	      `Original targetRole: ${targetRole}`,
   371	      `Full original record: ${preservedOriginal}`
   372	    ].join(' | ')
   373	  };
   374	
   375	  // 5. Validate the entry before writing
   376	  const errors = validateEntry(importedEntry);
   377	  if (errors.length) {
   378	    console.error('STOP: migrated entry fails schema validation — not writing (data preserved in policy/ledger.json):');
   379	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   380	    process.exit(1);
   381	  }
   382	
   383	  // 6. Append to ledger
   384	  const newLen = writeLedger(existing, importedEntry);
   385	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Migrated entry written to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   386	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${existing.length} → ${newLen}`);
   387	
   388	  // 7. Leave policy/ledger.json as a stub pointing to the canonical ledger
   389	  const stub = {
   390	    _stub:      true,
   391	    _canonical: 'builder-control/ledger.json',
   392	    _migrated:  new Date().toISOString(),
   393	    _note:      'This file is a stub. The single record originally here was migrated to builder-control/ledger.json as entryId LED-LEGACY-IMPORT-0001. Do not delete this file or edit builder-control/ledger.json directly — use ledger-writer.cjs --append.'
   394	  };
   395	  fs.writeFileSync(POLICY_LEDGER, JSON.stringify(stub, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   396	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] policy/ledger.json → stub written (canonical: builder-control/ledger.json)`);
   397	
   398	  // 8. Print the migrated entry for anti-theater verification
   399	  console.log('\n[ledger-writer] Migrated entry:');
   400	  console.log(JSON.stringify(importedEntry, null, 2));
   401	
   402	  process.exit(0);
   403	}
   404	
   405	// ─── CLI dispatch ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   406	// Guarded so this file can be require()d as a module. It previously ran the CLI
   407	// (and process.exit'd) on import, which meant any code trying to reuse
   408	// appendAtomic() killed itself printing a usage message.
   409	const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   410	const cmd  = args[0];
   411	
   412	if (require.main !== module) {
   413	  // imported as a library — export only, run nothing
   414	} else if (cmd === '--append') {
   415	  const entryPath = args[1];
   416	  if (!entryPath) {
   417	    console.error('Usage: node ledger-writer.cjs --append <entry.json>');
   418	    process.exit(2);
   419	  }
   420	  appendEntry(entryPath);
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * ledger-atomicity.test.cjs — red proofs for the canonical evidence ledger.
     4	 *
     5	 * FIXTURE ISOLATION — why this file no longer touches the real ledger
     6	 * The first version of these tests asserted absolute counts against
     7	 * builder-control/ledger.json, the live production ledger that gate.cjs,
     8	 * preflight.cjs and three other suites also append to. Two failures followed,
     9	 * and independent verification caught both:
    10	 *
    11	 *   - under a PARALLEL runner, other suites' legitimate appends landed between
    12	 *     the `before` and `after` reads, so "6 concurrent appends" measured 7 and
    13	 *     "1 deduped entry" measured 2. The implementation was correct the whole
    14	 *     time; the measurement was taken against a shared moving target.
    15	 *
    16	 *   - worse, the old withBackup() helper RESTORED the ledger afterwards, which
    17	 *     silently destroyed entries other suites had legitimately written. A test
    18	 *     verifying that the ledger never loses entries was itself losing them.
    19	 *
    20	 * Every case now runs against a private ledger in a temp directory via
    21	 * AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE. No shared state, no restore, nothing to contaminate.
    22	 */
    23	'use strict';
    24	const assert = require('assert');
    25	const fs = require('fs');
    26	const os = require('os');
    27	const path = require('path');
    28	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    29	
    30	const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');
    31	const BC = path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control');
    32	const WRITER = path.join(BC, 'ledger-writer.cjs');
    33	const REAL_LEDGER = path.join(BC, 'ledger.json');
    34	
    35	let passed = 0;
    36	function test(n, fn) {
    37	  try { fn(); passed++; console.log(`ok   ${n}`); }
    38	  catch (e) { console.error(`FAIL ${n}: ${e.message}`); process.exitCode = 1; }
    39	}
    40	
    41	const TMP = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(fs.realpathSync(os.tmpdir()), 'aegis-ledger-'));
    42	function cleanup() { try { fs.rmSync(TMP, { recursive: true, force: true }); } catch {} }
    43	process.on('exit', cleanup);
    44	process.on('SIGTERM', () => { cleanup(); process.exit(1); });
    45	process.on('SIGINT', () => { cleanup(); process.exit(1); });
    46	
    47	// A fresh, private ledger per case. Isolation is per-test, not per-file, so one
    48	// case can never see another's entries either.
    49	let caseNo = 0;
    50	function isolatedLedger() {
    51	  const p = path.join(TMP, `ledger-${++caseNo}.json`);
    52	  fs.writeFileSync(p, '[]\n');
    53	  return p;
    54	}
    55	
    56	const entry = (id, extra = {}) => ({
    57	  entryId: id,
    58	  ts: new Date().toISOString(),
    59	  agentId: 'claude-code',
    60	  gate: 'engineering-os',
    61	  status: 'PASS',
    62	  notes: 'atomicity fixture',
    63	  ...extra,
    64	});
    65	
    66	function appendViaCli(ledger, e) {
    67	  const f = path.join(TMP, `entry-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}.json`);
    68	  fs.writeFileSync(f, JSON.stringify(e));
    69	  try {
    70	    return spawnSync('node', [WRITER, '--append', f], {
    71	      cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', env: { ...process.env, AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE: ledger },
    72	    });
    73	  } finally { try { fs.unlinkSync(f); } catch {} }
    74	}
    75	const read = (l) => JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(l, 'utf8'));
    76	
    77	console.log('AEGIS ledger — atomicity and idempotency red proofs');
    78	
    79	// Captured before anything runs. The final case proves this file left the real
    80	// production ledger byte-identical.
    81	const REAL_LEDGER_BEFORE = fs.existsSync(REAL_LEDGER) ? fs.readFileSync(REAL_LEDGER, 'utf8') : null;
    82	
    83	test('RED: concurrent appends do NOT lose an entry', () => {
    84	  const L = isolatedLedger();
    85	  const N = 6;
    86	  const files = [];
    87	  for (let i = 0; i < N; i++) {
    88	    const f = path.join(TMP, `conc-${i}.json`);
    89	    fs.writeFileSync(f, JSON.stringify(entry(`LED-CONC-${i}`)));
    90	    files.push(f);
    91	  }
    92	  const cmd = files.map((f) => `AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE=${JSON.stringify(L)} node ${JSON.stringify(WRITER)} --append ${JSON.stringify(f)} >/dev/null 2>&1 &`).join(' ') + ' wait';
    93	  const r = spawnSync('bash', ['-c', cmd], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 60000 });
    94	  assert.ok(r.status === 0 || r.status === null, 'writers did not complete');
    95	
    96	  const after = read(L);
    97	  assert.strictEqual(after.length, N,
    98	    `expected exactly ${N} entries in an isolated ledger, got ${after.length}`);
    99	  for (let i = 0; i < N; i++) {
   100	    assert.ok(after.some((x) => x.entryId === `LED-CONC-${i}`), `entry ${i} was lost`);
   101	  }
   102	});
   103	
   104	test('RED: a repeated operationId is a NO-OP, not a duplicate', () => {
   105	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   106	  const opId = 'OP-IDEMPOTENT';
   107	  const r1 = appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-IDEM-A', { operationId: opId, plane: 'INTEGRATION', attempt: 1, result: 'ok' }));
   108	  assert.strictEqual(r1.status, 0, r1.stderr);
   109	  const r2 = appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-IDEM-B', { operationId: opId, plane: 'INTEGRATION', attempt: 2, result: 'ok' }));
   110	  assert.strictEqual(r2.status, 0, 'a retry must succeed, not error');
   111	  assert.ok(/NO-OP/.test(r2.stdout), 'the retry must be reported as absorbed');
   112	
   113	  const after = read(L);
   114	  assert.strictEqual(after.length, 1, `a retried operation must not create a second entry (got ${after.length})`);
   115	  assert.strictEqual(after.filter((e) => e.operationId === opId).length, 1);
   116	});
   117	
   118	test('RED: a duplicate entryId is refused', () => {
   119	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   120	  assert.strictEqual(appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-DUP')).status, 0);
   121	  const r = appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-DUP'));
   122	  assert.notStrictEqual(r.status, 0, 'a duplicate entryId must be refused');
   123	  assert.ok(/already exists/.test(r.stderr + r.stdout));
   124	  assert.strictEqual(read(L).length, 1);
   125	});
   126	
   127	test('RED: a stale in-memory base cannot overwrite newer on-disk entries', () => {
   128	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   129	  // The exact defect: hold a stale snapshot, let another writer append, then
   130	  // append using that stale snapshot as the base.
   131	  appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-FIRST'));
   132	  const stale = read(L);
   133	  appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-OTHER'));
   134	  const W = require('child_process');
   135	  W.spawnSync('node', ['-e',
   136	    `require(${JSON.stringify(WRITER)}).appendAtomic(${JSON.stringify(entry('LED-STALE'))})`],
   137	    { cwd: ROOT, env: { ...process.env, AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE: L }, encoding: 'utf8' });
   138	
   139	  const after = read(L);
   140	  assert.ok(after.some((e) => e.entryId === 'LED-OTHER'),
   141	    "the other writer's entry must survive — this is exactly what the old code destroyed");
   142	  assert.ok(after.some((e) => e.entryId === 'LED-STALE'));
   143	  assert.strictEqual(after.length, 3);
   144	  assert.strictEqual(stale.length, 1, 'the stale snapshot is genuinely stale');
   145	});
   146	
   147	test('the ledger schema accepts FAILED integration events with idempotency keys', () => {
   148	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   149	  const r = appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-FAILED', {
   150	    status: 'FAILED', plane: 'INTEGRATION',
   151	    operationId: 'OP-FAIL', correlationId: 'CORR-1',
   152	    attempt: 3, result: 'notion 502 after 3 attempts',
   153	  }));
   154	  assert.strictEqual(r.status, 0, `a conforming FAILED integration event must append: ${r.stderr}`);
   155	  assert.strictEqual(read(L)[0].status, 'FAILED');
   156	});
   157	
   158	test('no lock or temp file is left behind after a successful append', () => {
   159	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   160	  appendViaCli(L, entry('LED-CLEAN'));
   161	  assert.ok(!fs.existsSync(L + '.lock'), 'lock file leaked');
   162	  const tmps = fs.readdirSync(TMP).filter((f) => f.includes('.tmp-'));
   163	  assert.deepStrictEqual(tmps, [], 'temp file leaked');
   164	});

   166	// ── CROSS-RUN CONTAMINATION PROOFS ─────────────────────────────────────────
   167	// These are the proofs that would have caught the reported failure.
   168	
   169	test('RED: cases are isolated from each other — no shared counter', () => {
   170	  const A = isolatedLedger();
   171	  const B = isolatedLedger();
   172	  appendViaCli(A, entry('LED-IN-A'));
   173	  appendViaCli(A, entry('LED-IN-A-2'));
   174	  appendViaCli(B, entry('LED-IN-B'));
   175	  assert.strictEqual(read(A).length, 2);
   176	  assert.strictEqual(read(B).length, 1, 'ledger B saw entries written to ledger A');
   177	  assert.ok(!read(B).some((e) => String(e.entryId).includes('IN-A')));
   178	});
   179	
   180	test('RED: a FOREIGN concurrent writer cannot perturb an isolated count', () => {
   181	  // This is the reported failure, reproduced deliberately: another process
   182	  // hammers the REAL ledger while this case measures its own. Before isolation
   183	  // the foreign writes inflated the count by exactly the number of foreign
   184	  // appends. Now they are invisible here.
   185	  const L = isolatedLedger();
   186	  const foreign = path.join(TMP, 'foreign.json');
   187	  fs.writeFileSync(foreign, JSON.stringify(entry('LED-FOREIGN')));
   188	  const foreignLedger = isolatedLedger();
   189	  const noise = spawnSync('bash', ['-c',
   190	    `for i in 1 2 3; do AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE=${JSON.stringify(foreignLedger)} node ${JSON.stringify(WRITER)} --append ${JSON.stringify(foreign)} >/dev/null 2>&1; done &
   191	     AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE=${JSON.stringify(L)} node ${JSON.stringify(WRITER)} --append ${JSON.stringify(foreign)} >/dev/null 2>&1
   192	     wait`], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 60000 });
   193	  void noise;
   194	  assert.strictEqual(read(L).length, 1,
   195	    'a foreign writer perturbed this ledger — isolation is not real');
   196	});
   197	
   198	test('RED: this suite never writes to the REAL production ledger', () => {
   199	  // The old version both measured and RESTORED the live ledger, destroying
   200	  // other suites' entries. Nothing here may touch it at all.
   201	  //
   202	  // NOTE ON THE ASSERTION ITSELF: the first version of this proof compared the
   203	  // real ledger byte-for-byte and failed under a parallel runner — because
   204	  // gate.test.cjs and dispatch-preflight.smoke.cjs LEGITIMATELY append to it at
   205	  // the same time. That was the identical mistake this whole repair is about:
   206	  // asserting on a shared global instead of on this suite's own contribution.
   207	  // The correct claim is narrow — no entry belonging to THIS suite may appear
   208	  // in the real ledger — and it holds whether or not anything else is running.
   209	  if (REAL_LEDGER_BEFORE === null) return;
   210	  const before = JSON.parse(REAL_LEDGER_BEFORE);
   211	  const now = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(REAL_LEDGER, 'utf8'));
   212	
   213	  const OURS = /^LED-(CONC-|IDEM-|DUP$|FIRST$|OTHER$|STALE$|FAILED$|CLEAN$|IN-A|IN-B$|FOREIGN$)/;
   214	  const ourStrays = now.filter((e) => OURS.test(String(e.entryId || '')));
   215	  assert.deepStrictEqual(ourStrays.map((e) => e.entryId), [],
   216	    'entries from this suite reached the real production ledger — isolation is broken');
   217	
   218	  // And nothing that existed before may have been removed. This is the
   219	  // destructive half of the old bug: the restore silently dropped entries.
   220	  const missing = before.filter((b) => !now.some((n) => n.entryId === b.entryId));
   221	  assert.deepStrictEqual(missing.map((e) => e.entryId), [],
   222	    'pre-existing ledger entries disappeared while this suite ran');
   223	  const strays = fs.readdirSync(BC).filter((f) =>
   224	    f.startsWith('.test-') || f.startsWith('ledger.json.tmp-') || f === 'ledger.json.lock');
   225	  assert.deepStrictEqual(strays, [], `stray fixtures left in builder-control/: ${strays.join(', ')}`);
   226	});
   227	
   228	test('RED: the ledger override cannot redirect evidence outside a temp dir', () => {
   229	  // Injectability must not become an evidence-hiding switch.
   230	  const r = spawnSync('node', ['-e', 'require(process.argv[1])', WRITER], {
   231	    cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8',
   232	    env: { ...process.env, AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE: path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control', 'decoy-ledger.json') },
   233	  });
   234	  assert.notStrictEqual(r.status, 0, 'a non-temp ledger override must be refused');
   235	  assert.ok(/must point inside/.test(r.stderr), `expected a containment refusal, got: ${r.stderr.slice(0, 160)}`);
   236	  assert.ok(!fs.existsSync(path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control', 'decoy-ledger.json')), 'a decoy ledger was created');
   237	});
   238	
   239	const failed = process.exitCode ? 'at least 1' : '0';
   240	console.log(`${passed} passed, ${failed} failed.`);
     1	{
     2	  "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#",
     3	  "$id": "builder-control/schemas/ledger-entry.schema.json",
     4	  "title": "Builder Control — Evidence Ledger Entry (append-only)",
     5	  "description": "ONE entry schema, consolidating the policy/ledger.json stub shape and the Gate-D runtime-evidence JSON shape (gate/status/trace_id/evidence_paths/notes). Every gate decision — PASS and BLOCK — appends one entry. A change with no ledger entry never happened. The ledger file is an array of these objects; entries are never mutated, only appended.",
     6	  "type": "object",
     7	  "required": [
     8	    "entryId",
     9	    "ts",

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	'use strict';
     3	
     4	const fs = require('fs');
     5	const path = require('path');
     6	
     7	const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
     8	const DEFAULT_CANON = path.join(__dirname, 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json');
     9	const EXIT_OK = 0;
    10	const EXIT_USAGE = 2;
    11	const EXIT_BLOCK = 3;
    12	const DATA_RANK = { PUBLIC: 0, INTERNAL: 1, CONFIDENTIAL: 2, RESTRICTED: 3 };
    13	
    14	function parse(argv) {
    15	  const out = {};
    16	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    17	    const a = argv[i];
    18	    if (a === '--task') out.task = argv[++i];
    19	    else if (a === '--canon') out.canon = argv[++i];
    20	    else if (a === '--data-class') out.dataClass = argv[++i];
    21	    else if (a === '--allow-metered') out.allowMetered = true;
    22	    else if (a === '--approved-by') out.approvedBy = argv[++i];
    23	    else if (a === '--cap-usd') out.capUsd = argv[++i];
    24	    else if (a === '--role') out.role = argv[++i];
    25	    else if (a === '--model') out.model = argv[++i];
    26	    else if (a === '--wants-delegation') out.wantsDelegation = true;
    27	    else if (a === '--json') out.json = true;
    28	    else if (a === '--validate') out.validate = true;
    29	    else if (a === '--list') out.list = true;
    30	    else out.unknown = a;
    31	  }
    32	  return out;
    33	}
    34	
    35	function readCanon(file) {
    36	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
    37	}
    38	
    39	function validate(canon) {
    40	  const errors = [];
    41	  if (!canon || typeof canon !== 'object') return ['canon must be an object'];
    42	  if (!Array.isArray(canon.taskTypes) || canon.taskTypes.length === 0) errors.push('taskTypes must be non-empty');
    43	  if (!Array.isArray(canon.tools) || canon.tools.length === 0) errors.push('tools must be non-empty');
    44	  if (canon.defaultMeteredBudgetUsd !== 0) errors.push('defaultMeteredBudgetUsd must remain 0; metered spend requires explicit authorization');
    45	
    46	  const taskIds = new Set();
    47	  const toolIds = new Set();
    48	  for (const task of canon.taskTypes || []) {
    49	    if (!task.taskId) errors.push('task missing taskId');
    50	    else if (taskIds.has(task.taskId)) errors.push(`duplicate taskId: ${task.taskId}`);
    51	    else taskIds.add(task.taskId);
    52	    if (!Array.isArray(task.preferredToolIds) || task.preferredToolIds.length === 0) errors.push(`${task.taskId || '(unknown task)'} has no preferredToolIds`);
    53	  }
    54	  for (const tool of canon.tools || []) {
    55	    if (!tool.toolId) errors.push('tool missing toolId');
    56	    else if (toolIds.has(tool.toolId)) errors.push(`duplicate toolId: ${tool.toolId}`);
    57	    else toolIds.add(tool.toolId);
    58	    if (!tool.availabilityEvidence || !tool.availabilityEvidence.result) errors.push(`${tool.toolId || '(unknown tool)'} has no availability evidence`);
    59	    for (const taskId of tool.taskIds || []) if (!taskIds.has(taskId)) errors.push(`${tool.toolId} references unknown taskId ${taskId}`);
    60	  }
    61	  for (const task of canon.taskTypes || []) {
    62	    for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds || []) if (!toolIds.has(toolId)) errors.push(`${task.taskId} prefers unknown tool ${toolId}`);
    63	  }
    64	  return errors;
    65	}
    66	
    67	
    68	// ── policy-based routing (MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json) ────────────────────────
    69	const POLICY_PATH = require('path').join(__dirname, 'MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json');
    70	
    71	function loadPolicy() {
    72	  const fs = require('fs');
    73	  if (!fs.existsSync(POLICY_PATH)) {
    74	    const e = new Error('MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json is missing. Refusing to route: an absent policy is not a permissive policy.');
    75	    e.refused = true; throw e;
    76	  }
    77	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(POLICY_PATH, 'utf8'));
    78	}
    79	
    80	// Metered execution needs a named human and a cap inside the hard ceiling.
    81	// Each condition exists because of a specific way spending goes wrong:
    82	// no name = nobody accountable; no cap = unbounded; cap above ceiling = the
    83	// ceiling was decorative.
    84	function meteredAuthorization(opts) {
    85	  let policy;
    86	  try { policy = loadPolicy(); }
    87	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: 'model routing policy unreadable — metered execution refused' }; }
    88	  const b = policy.budgets || {};
    89	  if (!opts.allowMetered) return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution has no explicit budget authorization' };
    90	  if (!opts.approvedBy || String(opts.approvedBy).trim() === '') {
    91	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --approved-by <named human>; an AI may not authorize spending on the owner\'s behalf' };
    92	  }
    93	  const cap = Number(opts.capUsd);
    94	  if (!Number.isFinite(cap) || cap <= 0) {
    95	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --cap-usd <number>; authorization without a cap is unbounded' };
    96	  }
    97	  const ceiling = Number(b.hardCeilingUsd);
    98	  if (Number.isFinite(ceiling) && cap > ceiling) {
    99	    return { ok: false, reason: `--cap-usd ${cap} exceeds the policy hard ceiling of ${ceiling}` };
   100	  }
   101	  return { ok: true, approvedBy: String(opts.approvedBy), capUsd: cap };
   102	}
   103	
   104	// Role routing: who performs a role, with the separation rules enforced.
   105	function routeRole(roleId, opts = {}) {
   106	  const policy = loadPolicy();
   107	  const role = (policy.roles || {})[roleId];
   108	  if (!role) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_ROLE', reason: `${roleId} is not a role in the routing policy.` };
   109	
   110	  const chosen = opts.model || role.default;
   111	
   112	  // A reviewer that is also the author is not a reviewer.
   113	  for (const other of role.mustDifferFrom || []) {
   114	    const otherRole = (policy.roles || {})[other];
   115	    const otherModel = (opts.assigned && opts.assigned[other]) || (otherRole && otherRole.default);
   116	    if (otherModel && otherModel === chosen) {
   117	      return {
   118	        ok: false, code: 'SELF_REVIEW_REFUSED',
   119	        reason: `${roleId} would be performed by "${chosen}", which is already performing "${other}". A model may not review its own work.`,
   120	      };
   121	    }
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  // Recursive delegation is off unless the role explicitly opts in.
   125	  const recursion = role.recursiveDelegation === true;
   126	  if (opts.wantsDelegation && !recursion) {
   127	    return {
   128	      ok: false, code: 'RECURSIVE_DELEGATION_REFUSED',
   129	      reason: `${roleId} requested sub-agent delegation, which is disabled by default. One bounded budget must not become an unbounded tree.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const model = (policy.models || {})[chosen];
   134	  if (!model) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_MODEL', reason: `${chosen} is not declared in the routing policy.` };
   135	
   136	  // Data sensitivity vetoes first, before capability or cost are considered.
   137	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   138	  const rank = (policy.dataClasses || {});
   139	  const want = rank[dataClass] && rank[dataClass].rank;
   140	  const max = rank[model.maxDataClass] && rank[model.maxDataClass].rank;
   141	  if (typeof want === 'number' && typeof max === 'number' && want > max) {
   142	    return {
   143	      ok: false, code: 'DATA_CLASS_REFUSED',
   144	      reason: `${dataClass} data may not be sent to ${chosen} (max ${model.maxDataClass}). Data sensitivity vetoes before capability or cost.`,
   145	    };
   146	  }
   147	
   148	  if (model.execution === 'METERED') {
   149	    const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   150	    if (!m.ok) return { ok: false, code: 'METERED_UNAUTHORIZED', reason: m.reason };
   151	  }
   152	
   153	  return {
   154	    ok: true, roleId, model: chosen, label: model.label,
   155	    execution: model.execution, dataClass,
   156	    bounds: role.bounds || null,
   157	    recursiveDelegation: recursion,
   158	    advisoryOnly: role.advisoryOnly === true,
   159	    mayApproveOwnWork: role.mayApproveOwnWork === true,
   160	  };
   161	}
   162	
   163	function route(canon, opts) {
   164	  const task = canon.taskTypes.find((t) => t.taskId === opts.task);
   165	  if (!task) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_TASK', reason: `Task ${opts.task} is not in the Tool Capability Canon.` };
   166	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   167	  if (!(dataClass in DATA_RANK)) return { ok: false, code: 'INVALID_DATA_CLASS', reason: `Unknown data class ${dataClass}.` };
   168	
   169	  const byId = new Map(canon.tools.map((t) => [t.toolId, t]));
   170	  const considered = [];
   171	  for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds) {
   172	    const tool = byId.get(toolId);
   173	    if (!tool) continue;
   174	    const reasons = [];
   175	    if (!tool.enabled || tool.availability === 'DISABLED') reasons.push('disabled');
   176	    if (tool.availability !== 'AVAILABLE') reasons.push(`availability=${tool.availability}`);
   177	    if (DATA_RANK[dataClass] > DATA_RANK[tool.maxDataClassification]) reasons.push(`data class ${dataClass} exceeds ${tool.maxDataClassification}`);
   178	    // CONFIRMED FINDING #5: a bare --allow-metered boolean used to authorize
   179	    // spending. Money now requires a name attached to the decision and a
   180	    // number attached to the spend, both bounded by the policy ceiling.
   181	    if (tool.costClass === 'METERED') {
   182	      const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   183	      if (!m.ok) reasons.push(m.reason);
   184	    }
   185	    considered.push({ toolId, eligible: reasons.length === 0, reasons });
   186	    if (reasons.length === 0) {
   187	      return {
   188	        ok: true,
   189	        taskId: task.taskId,
   190	        toolId: tool.toolId,
   191	        label: tool.label,
   192	        executionMode: tool.executionModes[0],
   193	        costClass: tool.costClass,
   194	        dataClass,
   195	        verification: tool.verification,
   196	        requiredInputs: task.requiredInputs || [],
   197	        requiredOutputs: task.requiredOutputs || [],
   198	        considered,
   199	      };
   200	    }
   201	  }
   202	  return {
   203	    ok: false,
   204	    code: 'SPECIALIST_REQUIRED',
   205	    reason: `No verified, enabled specialist can execute ${task.taskId}. Generalist fallback is ${task.generalistFallback}.`,
   206	    taskId: task.taskId,
   207	    considered,
   208	  };
   209	}
   210	
   211	function main() {
   212	  const opts = parse(process.argv.slice(2));
   213	  if (opts.unknown) {
   214	    console.error(`Unknown argument: ${opts.unknown}`);
   215	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   216	  }
   217	  const canonPath = path.resolve(ROOT, opts.canon || path.relative(ROOT, DEFAULT_CANON));
   218	  let canon;
   219	  try { canon = readCanon(canonPath); }
   220	  catch (e) { console.error(`Cannot read canon ${canonPath}: ${e.message}`); return EXIT_USAGE; }
   221	  const errors = validate(canon);
   222	  if (errors.length) {
   223	    console.error('TOOL CANON INVALID');
   224	    for (const error of errors) console.error(`- ${error}`);
   225	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   226	  }
   227	  if (opts.validate) {
   228	    console.log(`TOOL CANON VALID — ${canon.taskTypes.length} task type(s), ${canon.tools.length} tool profile(s), default metered budget $${canon.defaultMeteredBudgetUsd}`);
   229	    return EXIT_OK;
   230	  }
   231	  if (opts.list) {
   232	    for (const task of canon.taskTypes) console.log(`${task.taskId}: ${task.preferredToolIds.join(', ')}; fallback=${task.generalistFallback}`);
   233	    return EXIT_OK;
   234	  }
   235	  if (!opts.task) {
   236	    console.error('Usage: tool-router.cjs --task <taskId> [--data-class INTERNAL] [--allow-metered] [--json]');
   237	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   238	  }
   239	  const result = route(canon, opts);
   240	  if (opts.json) console.log(JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));
   241	  else if (result.ok) {
   242	    console.log(`ROUTE: ${result.toolId} (${result.executionMode}; ${result.costClass})`);
   243	    console.log(`TASK: ${result.taskId}`);
   244	    console.log(`DATA: ${result.dataClass}`);
   245	  } else {
   246	    console.log(`ROUTE: BLOCKED — ${result.code}`);
   247	    console.log(result.reason);
   248	    for (const item of result.considered || []) console.log(`- ${item.toolId}: ${item.reasons.join('; ') || 'eligible'}`);
   249	  }
   250	  return result.ok ? EXIT_OK : EXIT_BLOCK;
   251	}
   252	
   253	module.exports = { routeRole, meteredAuthorization, loadPolicy };
   254	
   255	if (require.main === module) process.exit(main());

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * model-routing.test.cjs — red proofs for policy-based model routing.
     4	 *
     5	 * Every case here asserts a REFUSAL. A router that says yes to everything
     6	 * passes a happy-path suite and provides no governance at all.
     7	 */
     8	'use strict';
     9	const assert = require('assert');
    10	const fs = require('fs');
    11	const path = require('path');
    12	const R = require('../tool-router.cjs');
    13	
    14	let passed = 0;
    15	function test(n, fn) {
    16	  try { fn(); passed++; console.log(`ok   ${n}`); }
    17	  catch (e) { console.error(`FAIL ${n}: ${e.message}`); process.exitCode = 1; }
    18	}
    19	console.log('AEGIS model routing — red proofs');
    20	
    21	test('the policy declares the roles the amendment requires', () => {
    22	  const p = R.loadPolicy();
    23	  for (const r of ['orchestrator', 'implementation-review', 'adversarial-review', 'repository-guardian']) {
    24	    assert.ok(p.roles[r], `missing role ${r}`);
    25	    assert.strictEqual(p.roles[r].mayApproveOwnWork, false, `${r} must not approve its own work`);
    26	  }
    27	  assert.strictEqual(p.roles.orchestrator.default, 'claude');
    28	  assert.strictEqual(p.roles['implementation-review'].default, 'codex');
    29	  assert.strictEqual(p.roles['adversarial-review'].default, 'grok');
    30	});
    31	
    32	test('data sensitivity vetoes BEFORE capability and cost', () => {
    33	  const p = R.loadPolicy();
    34	  assert.deepStrictEqual(p.vetoOrder, ['dataSensitivity', 'capability', 'availability', 'cost']);
    35	  const r = R.routeRole('implementation-review', { dataClass: 'SENSITIVE' });
    36	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false);
    37	  assert.strictEqual(r.code, 'DATA_CLASS_REFUSED');
    38	});
    39	
    40	test('RED: a bare --allow-metered does NOT authorize spending', () => {
    41	  const r = R.routeRole('adversarial-review', { allowMetered: true });
    42	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false, 'a boolean must not unlock money');
    43	  assert.strictEqual(r.code, 'METERED_UNAUTHORIZED');
    44	  assert.ok(/named human/i.test(r.reason));
    45	});
    46	
    47	test('RED: metered without a cap is refused even with a named human', () => {
    48	  const r = R.routeRole('adversarial-review', { allowMetered: true, approvedBy: 'Marc Papineau' });
    49	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false);
    50	  assert.ok(/cap/i.test(r.reason), 'must demand a cap');
    51	});
    52	
    53	test('RED: a cap above the policy hard ceiling is refused', () => {
    54	  const ceiling = R.loadPolicy().budgets.hardCeilingUsd;
    55	  const r = R.routeRole('adversarial-review', { allowMetered: true, approvedBy: 'Marc', capUsd: ceiling + 1 });
    56	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false);
    57	  assert.ok(/ceiling/i.test(r.reason), 'a ceiling that can be exceeded inline is not a ceiling');
    58	});
    59	
    60	test('a named human plus a cap inside the ceiling routes, carrying its bounds', () => {
    61	  const r = R.routeRole('adversarial-review', { allowMetered: true, approvedBy: 'Marc Papineau', capUsd: 2 });
    62	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, true);
    63	  assert.strictEqual(r.model, 'grok');
    64	  // The bounds the Product Owner specified must survive into the route.
    65	  assert.strictEqual(r.bounds.maxTurns, 1);
    66	  assert.strictEqual(r.bounds.maxRetries, 0);
    67	  assert.strictEqual(r.bounds.subagents, false);
    68	  assert.strictEqual(r.bounds.webAccess, false);
    69	});
    70	
    71	test('RED: a model may not review its own work', () => {
    72	  const r = R.routeRole('implementation-review', { model: 'claude' });
    73	  assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false);
    74	  assert.strictEqual(r.code, 'SELF_REVIEW_REFUSED');
    75	});
    76	
    77	test('RED: recursive delegation is refused by default for every role', () => {
    78	  for (const role of ['orchestrator', 'implementation-review', 'adversarial-review']) {
    79	    const r = R.routeRole(role, { wantsDelegation: true, allowMetered: true, approvedBy: 'M', capUsd: 1 });
    80	    assert.strictEqual(r.ok, false, `${role} allowed delegation`);
    81	    assert.strictEqual(r.code, 'RECURSIVE_DELEGATION_REFUSED');
    82	  }
    83	});
    84	
    85	test('RED: fallback exhaustion REFUSES rather than falling back to the author', () => {
    86	  const p = R.loadPolicy();
    87	  assert.strictEqual(p.fallbacks.fallbackExhaustedBehaviour, 'REFUSE');
    88	  assert.ok(!p.fallbacks['implementation-review'].includes('claude'),
    89	    'the orchestrator must never be a review fallback — self-review is most tempting exactly when the reviewer is down');
    90	});
    91	
    92	test('RED: a missing policy file refuses rather than routing permissively', () => {
    93	  const live = path.join(__dirname, '..', 'MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json');
    94	  const hidden = live + '.test-hidden';
    95	  const restore = () => { try { if (fs.existsSync(hidden)) fs.renameSync(hidden, live); } catch {} };
    96	  process.on('exit', restore);
    97	  fs.renameSync(live, hidden);
    98	  let threw = false;
    99	  try { R.routeRole('orchestrator', {}); } catch (e) { threw = /absent policy is not a permissive policy/.test(e.message); }
   100	  finally { restore(); }
   101	  assert.ok(threw, 'an absent policy must refuse');
   102	  assert.ok(fs.existsSync(live), 'policy must be restored');
   103	});
   104	
   105	test('repository-guardian is advisory and cannot approve', () => {
   106	  const p = R.loadPolicy();
   107	  assert.strictEqual(p.roles['repository-guardian'].advisoryOnly, true);
   108	  assert.strictEqual(p.roles['repository-guardian'].mayApproveOwnWork, false);
   109	});
   110	
   111	const failed = process.exitCode ? 'at least 1' : '0';
   112	console.log(`${passed} passed, ${failed} failed.`);
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	'use strict';
     3	
     4	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
     5	const fs = require('fs');
     6	const os = require('os');
     7	const path = require('path');
     8	
     9	const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');
    10	const CLI = path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control', 'tool-router.cjs');
    11	const REAL = path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control', 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json');
    12	const TMP = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'tool-router-test-'));
    13	process.on('exit', () => { try { fs.rmSync(TMP, { recursive: true, force: true }); } catch {} });
    14	
    15	let passed = 0;
    16	let failed = 0;
    17	
    18	function run(args) {
    19	  const r = spawnSync('node', [CLI, ...args], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
    20	  return { exit: r.status, out: `${r.stdout || ''}${r.stderr || ''}` };
    21	}
    22	
    23	function expect(name, args, exit, contains) {
    24	  const r = run(args);
    25	  const ok = r.exit === exit && [].concat(contains).every((s) => r.out.includes(s));
    26	  if (ok) { passed++; console.log(`ok   ${name}`); }
    27	  else { failed++; console.error(`FAIL ${name}\n${r.out}`); }
    28	}
    29	
    30	function fixture(mutator) {
    31	  const canon = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(REAL, 'utf8'));
    32	  mutator(canon);
    33	  const file = path.join(TMP, `canon-${Date.now()}-${Math.random()}.json`);
    34	  fs.writeFileSync(file, JSON.stringify(canon, null, 2));
    35	  return file;
    36	}
    37	
    38	console.log('AEGIS Tool Router — rule fixtures');
    39	
    40	expect('real canon validates', ['--validate'], 0, ['TOOL CANON VALID', 'default metered budget $0']);
    41	expect('video blocks while OpenArt is unverified', ['--task', 'media.video-generate'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'openart-video', 'UNVERIFIED']);
    42	expect('advertising blocks rather than falling back to Claude', ['--task', 'marketing.advertising'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'advertising-specialist-slot', 'disabled']);
    43	expect('unknown task refuses routing', ['--task', 'something.unknown'], 3, 'UNKNOWN_TASK');
    44	
    45	const openArtAvailable = fixture((canon) => {
    46	  const tool = canon.tools.find((t) => t.toolId === 'openart-video');
    47	  tool.availability = 'AVAILABLE';
    48	  tool.costClass = 'INCLUDED';
    49	});
    50	expect('verified OpenArt is selected for video', ['--canon', openArtAvailable, '--task', 'media.video-generate'], 0, ['ROUTE: openart-video', 'DESKTOP_ASSISTED']);
    51	
    52	const meteredOpenArt = fixture((canon) => {
    53	  const tool = canon.tools.find((t) => t.toolId === 'openart-video');
    54	  tool.availability = 'AVAILABLE';
    55	  tool.costClass = 'METERED';
    56	});
    57	expect('metered tool blocks under the zero-dollar default', ['--canon', meteredOpenArt, '--task', 'media.video-generate'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'no explicit budget authorization']);
    58	// CONFIRMED FINDING #5 tightened this contract: --allow-metered alone no
    59	// longer authorizes spending. It now additionally requires a named human and a
    60	// cap inside the policy ceiling, so this case asserts BOTH halves — the bare
    61	// boolean is refused, and the fully-authorized form routes.
    62	expect('RED: --allow-metered ALONE no longer authorizes metered execution', ['--canon', meteredOpenArt, '--task', 'media.video-generate', '--allow-metered'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'named human']);
    63	expect('metered routes only with a named human and a cap', ['--canon', meteredOpenArt, '--task', 'media.video-generate', '--allow-metered', '--approved-by', 'Marc Papineau', '--cap-usd', '2'], 0, ['ROUTE: openart-video', 'METERED']);
    64	expect('RED: a cap above the policy ceiling is refused', ['--canon', meteredOpenArt, '--task', 'media.video-generate', '--allow-metered', '--approved-by', 'Marc Papineau', '--cap-usd', '9999'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'ceiling']);
    65	expect('data ceiling is enforced', ['--canon', openArtAvailable, '--task', 'media.video-generate', '--data-class', 'CONFIDENTIAL'], 3, ['SPECIALIST_REQUIRED', 'exceeds INTERNAL']);
    66	
    67	console.log(`${passed} passed, ${failed} failed.`);
    68	process.exit(failed ? 1 : 0);
